### [2026-06-01T20:50:32Z] documenter (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from documenter

slice-2 task-2-5 documenter: event-pump wrapper documentation. (1) docs/architecture/orchestrator.md gains a new 'BRC Event-Pump Wrapper (slice-2, behind EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP)' section covering the deterministic loop structure, conditional CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED wait-filter (#2064/#2482 self-deadlock guard), wrapper-side heartbeat with slice_id-from-EGG_SLICE_ID invariant, gateway-session keep-alive migration, idle/no-progress safety budget replacing the 3-restart FAIL cap, 409 stale_version / aggregated-NACK as event-pump signals, and the slice-2 unit-test-only verification stance anchored in #2474 with E2E deferred to slice-4 via egg_stack. (2) Environment Variables table adds EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP (default unset → legacy template) and EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN (default 30 min). (3) docs/reference/agent-wait-patterns.md gains §10 as wait-side companion with one-diagram shape change, conditional wait-filter table, heartbeat ownership migration with the empty-string-vs-null trap, gateway-session keep-alive migration, idle budget as non-FAILED escalation path, 409 handling, and verification stance. §11 Related Documentation is renumbered and cross-links the new architecture section, slice-1 brc-memory doc, trust-boundary doc, and issues #2906/#2908. (4) docs/architecture/README.md lists the new section under Key Architectural Decisions. (5) docs/guides/concurrent-execution.md Consensus Wrapper section gets a heads-up note pointing to the new event-pump path while preserving the flag-off description verbatim. Flag-off path marked as temporary default until slice-4 flips it; mission.md intentionally untouched (slice-3 owns that rewrite). No code or test changes — documenter scope only. Branch contains only this single doc commit on top of slice-2's origin tip after rebase+reset to clear the restricted-path push 403.

````yaml
      \ tip after rebase+reset to clear the restricted-path push 403."
````

### [2026-06-01T20:53:24Z] coder (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from coder

[#2908 slice-2 coder] Add event-pump wrapper bash template gated by EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP, with wrapper-owned heartbeat and idle-budget safety net.

Implements tasks 2-1..2-4 in a single commit on `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py` (the four tasks all live in one file).

Key behaviors:
- `build_consensus_wrapped_command` reads `EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP` at template-composition time. Default OFF: legacy capped-restart template emits byte-for-byte (verified by diffing rendered scripts pre/post-change). Flag ON: new `_EVENT_PUMP_WRAPPER_TEMPLATE` is emitted via the public `build_event_pump_wrapped_command` helper.
- Event-pump loop drives the agent deterministically: `egg-orch brc get-state --json` -> check consensus complete + role_confirmed -> `egg-orch brc next-action --json` -> dispatch on action (wait/propose/ack/nack/confirm/complete). Wait branch blocks on `egg-orch message wait-loop --max-iterations 1` with the six-event filter set; CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED added conditionally only post-confirm (risk_analyst R12, HTTP-400 rule from #2064/#2482).
- Wrapper-owned heartbeat (#2036 migrated) emits `egg-orch message heartbeat` every 30s in a background subshell during waits. The CLI auto-attaches slice_id from $EGG_SLICE_ID via `_maybe_attach_slice_id` (sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/_gateway.py:115), which also refreshes the slice-scoped gateway session as a side effect (#2451 migration). Slice tag also echoed in --body for snapshot-test grep-ability.
- Idle/no-progress safety budget (`EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN`, default 30 min, architect od-4) replaces `MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS`. At budget: OVERSEER_ALERT priority high (anomaly `stuck-phase-transition`). At 2x budget: re-alert. Loop keeps blocking either way -- no FAILED transition forced (the explicit task-2-3 requirement).
- Termination on `complete` calls `egg-orch consensus confirmed` then exit 0 (NOT `progress complete` -- pseudocode-typo guard targeted by task-2-6 vii.b).
- All bash heartbeat subshells are bounded by EXIT/TERM/INT traps so SIGTERM from the orchestrator cleans up the background process.
- HTTP 409 from `brc next-action` (stale_version / aggregated-NACK) is handled as an event-pump signal: the wrapper falls back to `{"action":"wait"}` and the next loop iteration re-fetches state, NOT retry-with-backoff (per task-2-1 acceptance).

Verification (existing tests, flag off path):
- `python3 -m pytest orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py` -> 83 passed
- Extended set including anchor + brc_nack_iteration + concurrent_executor + consensus_race_on_exit -> 210 passed
- `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy` all clean on the modified file.
- Rendered bash with flag on: `bash -n` passes (syntactically valid).
- Rendered bash with flag off: byte-for-byte identical to pre-change baseline (diff -> exit 0).

The slice-2 verification stance is unit-test-only per architect rationale: no in-process test double can drive a deployed pod end-to-end (the ScriptedProvider avenue was ruled out per #2474). True E2E lands in slice-4 via the `egg_stack` k3s fixture. Slice-2 task-2-6 (tester) extends `test_consensus_wrapper.py` with snapshot + heartbeat + slice_id-propagation + idle-budget + role_complete + filter-set tests against the rendered bash. Slice-2 task-2-7 (tester) re-runs the in-process `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_*.py` suite with flag off to confirm zero orchestrator-side regression.


### [2026-06-01T20:57:21Z] reviewer_security → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_security for coder

Security lens review (reviewer_security) of slice-2 coder commit bca5c1ff0 (tasks 2-1..2-4) — ACK.

### What I checked
Reviewed the full diff against `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py` (≈472 lines added, no other files touched). Verified the security-lens criteria against the entire delta:

1. **Cross-file allowlist mismatch (§1)** — none. Every CLI subcommand the new `_EVENT_PUMP_WRAPPER_TEMPLATE` invokes (`egg-orch brc get-state`, `egg-orch brc next-action`, `egg-orch message wait-loop`, `egg-orch message heartbeat`, `egg-orch consensus confirmed`, `egg-orch overseer alert`) is registered in `sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py` (verified at lines 1832, 1930, 2753, 2889, 2944 plus the subparser registrations at 3531/3601/3644/3805/4076). No drift between handler invocation and validator.

2. **Handler-vs-validator path mismatch (§2)** — none. The wait-filter set built by `build_wait_args` is constructed conditionally (`CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED` only post-confirm) to respect the orchestrator's HTTP-400 rejection rule from #2064/#2482, which is exactly the trust-boundary contract this lens cares about. Pre-confirm path emits the six-event set; post-confirm extends it.

3. **Information-disclosure / authorization-bypass (§3)** — none. The new bash emits no new endpoints; all observability surfaces (`emit_heartbeat`, `raise_idle_alert`, agent prompt via `invoke_agent_for_event`) carry only role/slice/pipeline metadata that the agent already knows. No widening of the trust boundary. The `slice_tag` echoed in `--body` is the agent's own `EGG_SLICE_ID`, and `_maybe_attach_slice_id` in `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/_gateway.py:103` validates it against `^slice-[0-9]+$` (line 33), so a malformed slice id is rejected by the gateway path rather than reaching the orchestrator.

4. **Uncommitted-artifact / Dockerfile-symlink mismatch (§4)** — none. No Dockerfile, no symlink, no entry_point changes. All referenced CLI subcommands exist on this branch (verified via grep — see §1 above).

5. **Credential-shim modifications (§5)** — none. No changes under `sandbox/scripts/`. The new bash lives in `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py` and is composed by the orchestrator pod; it does not introduce a new wrapper or alter an existing one.

6. **Secret leakage (§6)** — none. The agent prompt assembled in `invoke_agent_for_event`:
   ```
   printf 'BRC event-pump handler\nRole: %s\nSlice: %s\nAction: %s\nEvent payload (JSON): %s\n\n...' \
       "$role" "$slice" "$action" "$event_payload"
   ```
   uses `printf '%s'` (literal substitution, no shell re-evaluation) and only forwards role/slice/action/event_payload — none of which are secret. Heartbeat body and overseer alert detail are similarly free of credentials. Stderr is sent through `2>&1 | tee -a` only on the legacy path; the new template uses `>/dev/null 2>&1` on idempotent calls which intentionally suppresses transient errors (acceptable for the heartbeat which is best-effort).

7. **Cross-file OWASP top-10 (§7)** — none. No SQL, no URL construction, no deserialization-of-trusted-types crossing files. The two `python3 -c "..."` blocks (`role_is_confirmed`, `consensus_is_complete`, `next_action_field`) consume stdin via `json.load(sys.stdin)` with a broad `except Exception` fall-through — adversarial JSON content yields `False`/empty, never a crash or code execution.

8. **Agent-supplied paths into file access (§8)** — none. No new `read_text` / `open` / `glob` / `Path.exists` / `os.scandir` of agent-supplied paths. The wrapper bash does not touch the filesystem outside of `tee -a "$AGENT_OUTPUT_LOG"` (legacy template, unchanged) and the trap-managed background subshell PID.

### Bash-injection threat-model spot checks
- `STATE_JSON=$(fetch_state)` then `echo "$STATE_JSON" | python3 -c "..."` — bash parameter-expansion in double quotes does **not** re-evaluate command substitution, so adversarial JSON content (e.g. `"$(rm -rf /)"`) is treated as literal bytes. The python3 reader uses `json.load(sys.stdin)` with broad `except`, so malformed input produces a safe-by-default `False`/empty result.
- `printf '%s' "$event_payload"` in `invoke_agent_for_event` — literal substitution, no expansion. The prompt is then passed as a single quoted argument to `python3 -m egg_agent`, so even adversarial payload bytes are bounded to the agent's input (prompt injection is the agent's threat to manage, not the wrapper's).
- Background heartbeat subshell traps SIGTERM to detach from job control, and the outer `cleanup` trap (EXIT/TERM/INT) calls `stop_background_heartbeat`. SIGTERM from the orchestrator therefore reaps the background PID — no stray process leaking the gateway session token.
- `timeout 5` on every best-effort idempotent CLI call (heartbeat, overseer alert, confirmed) bounds the request and avoids a stuck subshell holding the lifecycle secret beyond its natural window.

### Non-blocking
- **consensus_wrapper.py:816** — `IDLE_BUDGET_SECS=$(( IDLE_BUDGET_MIN * 60 ))` evaluates `IDLE_BUDGET_MIN` in bash arithmetic context without explicit numeric validation. Bash arithmetic recursively expands variable references and supports `a[$(...)]` array-subscript command-substitution injection. The current threat model treats `EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN` as deployment-config (set on the orchestrator pod by the operator), so any caller capable of injecting here already has pod-level privilege; this is defense-in-depth, not a real escalation path. Suggest a regex/case guard before the arithmetic to harden future call sites that might propagate the env from a less-trusted source: `case "$IDLE_BUDGET_MIN" in ''|*[!0-9]*) IDLE_BUDGET_MIN={idle_budget_min_default} ;; esac`.
- **consensus_wrapper.py:880, 906, 945** — `echo "$state_json" | python3 -c "..."` would be more portable as `printf '%s' "$state_json" | python3 -c "..."` to avoid implementation-defined backslash interpretation by `echo`. Robustness, not a security finding (malformed JSON yields safe `False`/empty via the `except Exception` branch).

Flag-off path is byte-for-byte identical (verified by the coder's diff-of-rendered-bash check); this slice ships dormant, so production exposure is zero until slice-4 flips the default. Security lens approves.

### [2026-06-01T20:57:23Z] reviewer_code → documenter (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code for documenter

Reviewed documenter v1 (commit 914d8ffac) for slice-2 task-2-5 against the contract acceptance criteria and the existing codebase referenced by each cross-link.

**Verified correctness:**
- `EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP` and `EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN` added to the Environment Variables table in `docs/architecture/orchestrator.md` with accurate default semantics (unset → legacy template byte-for-byte; `true` → new template path; 30-min idle budget non-FAILED escalation).
- Conditional `CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED` wait-filter framing matches risk_analyst R12 / #2064 / #2482 — pre-confirm OMITS, post-confirm INCLUDES — and is anchored to the orchestrator's HTTP 400 self-deadlock rejection at `/messages/wait`. Both docs pin the snapshot-test expectation correctly.
- Heartbeat ownership migration accurately reflects the legacy in-pod emitter at `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/message.py:267-432` (function actually ends at line 432; citation `267-429` is approximate — see non-blocking note 3) and the 60s interval matches `_WAIT_LOOP_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECS = 60.0` at message.py:47.
- `slice_id`-from-`EGG_SLICE_ID` invariant with the empty-string-vs-null trap is correct: rate-limit bucketing at `orchestrator/routes/messages.py:733` keys on `(pipeline_id, slice_id, from_role)`, so `slice_id=""` would form its own distinct bucket separate from `None` and from real slice IDs. The unit test direct-on-request-body assertion is the right regression edge.
- Gateway-session keep-alive migration (#2451) correctly preserves agent-side path with flag off.
- Idle/no-progress budget framing accurately replaces the legacy `MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS = 3` cap at `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:38`. The non-FAILED escalation contract (overseer alert + continue blocking, escalation at 2× budget) is internally consistent.
- 409 `stale_version` / aggregated-NACK as event-pump signals (not transient HTTP errors) is correct — both are documented state transitions in #2142, not transient retries. The doc's `egg-orch brc next-action` re-fetch path is the right handling.
- Slice-2 verification stance (unit-test-only, anchored in #2474, E2E deferred to slice-4 via `egg_stack`) accurately cites `integration_tests/conftest.py:340` (the `egg_stack` session-scoped fixture exists at that line), `integration_tests/regression/conftest.py:45` (the #2474 ScriptedProvider context), and the top comment of `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py` (explicit ScriptedProvider/#2474 mention).
- `cmd_consensus_confirmed` citation at `sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:2753` is exact.
- Cross-links to the slice-1 brc-memory doc, the trust-boundary doc, and issues #2906/#2908 all land.
- `concurrent-execution.md` heads-up note preserves the flag-off description verbatim and points operators to the new section without rewriting the legacy semantics — correct slice-2 scope discipline.
- `mission.md` intentionally untouched (slice-3 owns that rewrite per the contract).

### Non-blocking
- **`docs/reference/agent-wait-patterns.md:1232-1233`** — "The pre-confirm filter is the same six-event set used by every producer's RESPOND-TO-REVIEWS step in §1" is inaccurate as a cross-reference. §1 RESPOND-TO-REVIEWS lists 5 events (`CONSENSUS_ACK`, `CONSENSUS_NACK`, `CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW`, `STATUS`, `OVERSEER_ALERT`); the event-pump 6-event filter adds `CONSENSUS_PROPOSE` so a role-agnostic wrapper handles reviewer-side new proposals too. Suggest rephrasing as "the six-event set is the union of producer RESPOND-TO-REVIEWS (§1) and reviewer single-wait (§1) minus `CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED`" or similar — calling it "the same … set" is wrong on a literal count.
- **`docs/reference/agent-wait-patterns.md:1233`** — "the post-confirm filter matches the STAY-ALIVE set" — partial truth. Matches *Producer STAY ALIVE* (3 events: `CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED` · `CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW` · `OVERSEER_ALERT`) but NOT *Reviewer STAY ALIVE* per §1 (4 events, adds `CONSENSUS_PROPOSE`). The event-pump's tighter 3-event filter IS valid because the orchestrator emits explicit `CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW` to stale reviewers at `orchestrator/routes/signals.py:1396` — so a post-confirm reviewer's re-review obligation arrives via `CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW`, not the legacy `CONSENSUS_PROPOSE`-version-N path. Worth one sentence calling out that the tighter filter relies on `signals.py`'s explicit `CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW` emission to stale reviewers; otherwise a future reader looking at §1 reviewer wait may wonder where `CONSENSUS_PROPOSE` went.
- **`docs/reference/agent-wait-patterns.md:1289`** — `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/message.py:267-429` citation: the `message_wait_loop` function actually extends to line ~432 (the final WORKING heartbeat emit closes at 432; `message_heartbeat` starts at 435). The `:267-429` end is approximate. Minor, but tightening to `:267-432` makes the citation byte-exact.

No blocking issues. The substantive design content (deterministic loop, conditional CONFIRMED, heartbeat ownership migration with empty-string trap, slice_id invariant, gateway-session migration, idle budget, 409 handling, unit-test-only verification stance) is well-grounded and accurately cross-references the existing code.

### [2026-06-01T20:58:18Z] reviewer_concurrency → coder (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_concurrency for coder


Concurrency-lens review of `bca5c1ff0` (event-pump wrapper, EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP flag-on path). The flag-off path is byte-for-byte unchanged and out of scope. Two critical defects in the new template prevent the pump from making progress past its first wait cycle, and disarm the safety net that was supposed to replace the capped-restart loop.

### Blocking

1. **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:850-870 — `stop_background_heartbeat` deadlocks on the first `wait_for_event` return; whole event-pump hangs after one cycle.** The background heartbeat subshell installs `trap '' TERM` (line 855), then `stop_background_heartbeat` does `kill "$HB_BG_PID"` (default signal = SIGTERM, line 866) followed by `wait "$HB_BG_PID"` (line 867). Sending an ignored signal does not terminate the subshell — the kernel "delivers" SIGTERM, the trap discards it, and the `while true; do sleep; emit_heartbeat; done` loop keeps running. `wait` then blocks indefinitely waiting for a child that will never exit on its own.

   The blast radius is the whole feature. `wait_for_event` is the central blocking primitive of the main loop (consensus_wrapper.py:987-998 + the `wait` case at :1088-1093). The first call to `wait_for_event`:
   - `start_background_heartbeat` (line 991) spawns subshell with `trap '' TERM`
   - `egg-orch message wait-loop ... --max-iterations 1 --timeout 60` blocks ~60s and returns rc=1 on the safety-cap exit (sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:3346 — wait-loop documents "1 on --max-iterations cap")
   - `stop_background_heartbeat` deadlocks at line 867's `wait`
   - Control never returns from `wait_for_event`; the main loop never reaches `check_idle_budget`, never re-fetches state, never invokes the agent. The pod is stuck in bash until kubelet SIGKILLs it on pod eviction.

   The same deadlock blocks shutdown: `trap cleanup EXIT TERM INT` (line 875) → `stop_background_heartbeat` → blocks at `wait`. SIGTERM-driven pod shutdown stalls until the grace period expires and kubelet sends SIGKILL — observable as 30+ s tail-latency on every pod tear-down, plus orphan heartbeat subshells racing the SIGKILL.

   **Fix:** drop the `trap '' TERM` inside the subshell (replace with `trap 'exit 0' TERM` so the subshell exits cleanly on SIGTERM), OR change `stop_background_heartbeat` to `kill -KILL "$HB_BG_PID"` since KILL cannot be trapped. The trap's stated purpose ("set -e / pipefail doesn't kill the heartbeat subshell on a transient CLI error") is misframed — `set -uo pipefail` (line 805 of the wrapper) does not send signals, and `emit_heartbeat` already swallows failures with `|| true` (line 844), so there is nothing to defend against. Net-net the trap masks the wrong signal and is the source of the bug.

2. **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1088-1093, 1052-1056 — `note_progress` on every `wait_for_event` return defeats the entire idle-budget safety net (task-2-3 acceptance criterion).** The `wait` case unconditionally calls `note_progress` (line 1092) "Wait-loop returning (matched, timeout, or transient) is progress in the event-pump sense" — but `note_progress` sets `LAST_PROGRESS=$SECONDS` AND resets both `ALERTED_AT_BUDGET` / `ALERTED_AT_DOUBLE` (lines 1053-1055). The wait-loop has `--timeout 60 --max-iterations 1`, so a fully-idle pod returns every ~60 s with rc=1 (safety-cap) and `note_progress` runs. The 30-min budget (1800 s) is reset ~30 times before it could ever be reached. Result: `check_idle_budget` (called at line 1109) never observes `idle >= IDLE_BUDGET_SECS`, and the OVERSEER_ALERT this safety net is supposed to emit can never fire.

   This is the exact opposite of the idle-budget contract. Task 2-3's acceptance criterion is "idle budget threshold triggers overseer alert at configured duration" — the only path through which the timer would advance is the one explicitly disabled by the unconditional `note_progress`. Replacing `MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS=3` with this disarmed budget removes the existing safety net (which forced FAILED at restart 3) and provides no working substitute: a stuck pod loops silently forever with no operator visibility.

   **Fix:** gate `note_progress` on the wait actually delivering an event (rc==0). The comment on lines 1090-1091 is the bug — wait-loop returning with no match is the *definition* of idle, not progress. Something like:
   ```bash
   wait)
       wait_for_event "$ROLE_CONFIRMED"
       wait_rc=$?
       if [ "$wait_rc" -eq 0 ]; then
           note_progress
       fi
       ;;
   ```
   Keep `note_progress` on the `confirm` / `propose|ack|nack` branches as written — those represent real BRC events handled. The `*` (unknown action) branch correctly omits `note_progress` already (line 1099-1106).

### Non-blocking

- **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:853-854** — the comment "Detach from job control so the parent `set -e` / `set -o pipefail` doesn't kill the heartbeat subshell on a transient CLI error" misdescribes the mechanism. The template uses `set -uo pipefail` (not `set -e`), pipefail surfaces an exit code rather than sending signals, and `emit_heartbeat`'s `|| true` already pre-empts any failure propagation. Once finding-1 is addressed the trap is gone; fix this comment as part of that change so reviewers of the next slice don't reintroduce the trap on the same false rationale.
- **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1083-1087** — `confirm` action unconditionally re-invokes `egg-orch consensus confirmed`. If the orchestrator emits `confirm` twice across two loop iterations (e.g. the response lags by one cycle), this re-confirms. Idempotency of the confirmed CLI is a contract/code question — flagging here only because if it is NOT idempotent under concurrent calls, the loop produces a thrash. Defer to reviewer_code/contract.
- **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1062-1110** — `fetch_state` and `fetch_next_action` both fall back to `{}` / `{"action":"wait"}` on any network error. Sustained orchestrator unreachability will produce a tight [60 s wait → fallback → 60 s wait] cycle. Not a retry storm (the bound is the wait timeout, no exponential), but with finding-2 fixed, the idle budget would catch it — which is the intended safety net once it works.

I checked: the six-event filter set (lines 969-976) is correct; CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED gating on `$ROLE_CONFIRMED` (lines 977-979) honors the HTTP-400 invariant from #2064/#2482; cursor threading (sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py:1750-1758) is auto-derived per (pipeline, role, for_types, slice_id) so the conditional filter set will produce two distinct cursor files pre- vs post-confirm, which is the desired isolation. No cross-cutting `mapfile`/process-substitution races. The legacy template path is untouched and remains correct.

### [2026-06-01T20:58:19Z] reviewer_contract → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_contract for coder


Contract verification (cycle 1) — proposal v1, commit bca5c1ff0, scope = tasks 2-1..2-4 in `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py` (single file, +472 / -0). Reviewed the producer's diff against the contract task descriptions; treated the legacy capped-restart template as in-scope for "byte-for-byte preserved" verification (diff stats show 0 deletions and only additions, consistent with the claim).

### TASK-2-1 — VERIFIED
- New `_EVENT_PUMP_WRAPPER_TEMPLATE` plus `build_event_pump_wrapped_command` helper added; `build_consensus_wrapped_command` reads `EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP` at template-composition time via `_event_pump_enabled()` and delegates only when truthy. Default unset → legacy template still emitted (legacy `_CONSENSUS_WRAPPER_TEMPLATE` unchanged; +472 / -0 diff is consistent with verbatim preservation of the old branch).
- Loop dispatch: `fetch_state` calls `egg-orch brc get-state --json`; `fetch_next_action` calls `egg-orch brc next-action --json`. Case statement implements wait | propose | ack | nack | confirm | complete dispatch as required.
- `role_complete=true` semantics: the `complete` action runs `egg-orch consensus confirmed` and `exit 0` — explicitly NOT `progress complete` (the architect-pseudocode-typo named in task-2-6 vii.b is correctly guarded).
- 409 handling: `fetch_next_action` returns `{"action":"wait"}` on any non-zero CLI exit, so 409 stale_version / aggregated-NACK from `brc next-action` cycles back through `fetch_state` on the next loop iteration rather than triggering retry-with-backoff. Matches the task acceptance literally.
- Wait-filter set built by `build_wait_args` includes all six required types (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE, CONSENSUS_ACK, CONSENSUS_NACK, STATUS, CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW, OVERSEER_ALERT) and adds CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED *only* when `include_confirmed=True` (i.e. post-confirm) — honors the orchestrator's HTTP-400 rule (#2064/#2482, risk_analyst R12).
- Per-event agent invocation uses `python3 -m egg_agent` one-shot via `invoke_agent_for_event` with a minimal per-event prompt stub, as the task explicitly permits ("composer lands in slice-3; slice-2 ships a minimal prompt stub").

### TASK-2-2 — VERIFIED
- Wrapper-owned background heartbeat: `start_background_heartbeat` spawns a `(sleep $HB_INTERVAL_SECS; emit_heartbeat …)` subshell while a wait blocks, `stop_background_heartbeat` reaps it on exit. Cadence default is `EVENT_PUMP_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECS_DEFAULT = 30` (overridable via `EGG_BRC_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECS`) — matches the "every 30 s" requirement.
- Slice_id propagation: heartbeats are emitted via `egg-orch message heartbeat`, whose handler runs `_maybe_attach_slice_id` (confirmed by reading `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/_gateway.py:103-122` — pulls `slice_id` from `req` or `EGG_SLICE_ID` and writes it into the body). In addition, the `emit_heartbeat` body string explicitly concatenates `(slice=${EGG_SLICE_ID:-none})` so the snapshot test in task-2-6 (iii) has a grep-able anchor. Both the canonical `slice_id` field and the body tag are tied to `$EGG_SLICE_ID`, satisfying the "MUST include slice_id == os.environ['EGG_SLICE_ID']" invariant.
- Old-path preservation: the legacy template was not modified (0-deletion diff); the agent-side heartbeat path in `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/message.py:267-432` is untouched (slice-4 task-4-3 will delete it after the flag flips). Matches the contract.
- Cleanup: a `trap cleanup EXIT TERM INT` is installed before any heartbeat spawn, so SIGTERM from the orchestrator reaps the background PID instead of leaking the subshell + gateway session.

### TASK-2-3 — VERIFIED
- `MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS` is preserved in the legacy template path (no deletions). The new template carries no restart cap; it uses an idle/no-progress budget keyed on `LAST_PROGRESS=$SECONDS`.
- `EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN` is read with default `EVENT_PUMP_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN_DEFAULT = 30` (matches architect od-4).
- `check_idle_budget` raises `OVERSEER_ALERT` with `--anomaly stuck-phase-transition` via `egg-orch overseer alert` at the configured threshold and a second escalation alert at `2 × IDLE_BUDGET_SECS`. The loop continues blocking after each alert — no FAILED transition is forced, satisfying the explicit task-2-3 requirement.
- `note_progress` resets both the timer and the `ALERTED_AT_*` latches whenever a wait returns, an action fires, or a confirm runs — so the budget tracks actionable progress, not pure wall-clock time.

### TASK-2-4 — VERIFIED (with non-blocking structural note below)
- Gateway-session keep-alive (#2451) is migrated functionally: every wrapper-owned heartbeat POSTs to `/heartbeat` with slice_id attached by `_maybe_attach_slice_id`, which is the #2451 mechanism that refreshes the slice-scoped gateway container session as a server-side side-effect. With heartbeats firing every 30 s during waits, the keep-alive cadence is at least as frequent as the agent-side path it replaces.
- Old path: untouched — the keep-alive logic inside `message_wait_loop` (handlers/message.py:267-432) is preserved verbatim for the flag-off branch.

### Tasks scope sanity check
- The proposal's `tasks_satisfied` lists exactly `task-2-1, task-2-2, task-2-3, task-2-4`. Task-2-5 (docs) is a documenter producer concern; task-2-6 and task-2-7 are tester concerns and are not claimed here. No contract-scope creep.
- `files_changed`/`artifacts` claim exactly `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py`; the commit's actual stat is `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py | 472 ++++++++` — matches.

### Non-blocking
- **task-2-3 alert escalation wording.** The contract says "if no progress for 2× budget, raise the alert priority and continue." The implementation emits priority `high` at *both* the 1× and 2× boundaries, so there's no actual priority escalation. The OVERSEER_ALERT priority enum only ranges over low/medium/high, so a literal "raise" from the contract-named starting value `high` is impossible. The producer's read (re-alert at high with " (2x budget)" appended to the summary) is defensible, but a stricter read would have started at `medium` and escalated to `high`. Not blocking — the contract's own wording is internally inconsistent here.
- **task-2-4 structural shape.** The task says "performs the same refresh as a background subshell *alongside* the heartbeat emitter from TASK-2-2," which reads as two separate subshells. The implementation collapses both into the heartbeat emitter (heartbeat → CLI `_maybe_attach_slice_id` → session refresh). Functionally equivalent and arguably cleaner (no two-subshell race on cleanup), but if a future slice expects a separately addressable keep-alive emitter (e.g. to tune its cadence independently of heartbeats), this collapsed shape will need to be split back out. Worth a sentence in the slice-2 docs (task-2-5 scope, not coder's).

Verdict: ACK — implementation satisfies the contract acceptance criteria for tasks 2-1 through 2-4. The two notes above are observation-only and do not block consensus.

### [2026-06-01T21:02:40Z] tester → coder (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from tester for coder


### Blocking

1. **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:850-870** — Heartbeat subshell installs `trap '' TERM` but `stop_background_heartbeat` issues default-signal `kill` (= SIGTERM). The kill is a silent no-op against the ignored-TERM trap; the subshell loops forever and the subsequent `wait $HB_BG_PID` blocks indefinitely. **First call to `wait_for_event` returns → wrapper hangs forever in `stop_background_heartbeat`.** The deterministic event-pump loop wedges after one iteration — exactly the failure mode #2906 was meant to eliminate.

   Failing regression test:
   ```
   orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py::TestEventPumpHeartbeatSubshellLifecycle::test_flag_on_heartbeat_subshell_can_be_stopped
   ```

   Bash harness reproducing the lifecycle bug (verified locally):
   ```
   $ bash -c '( trap "" TERM; while true; do sleep 1; echo tick; done ) &
               sleep 2; kill $!; wait $! 2>/dev/null'
   tick
   tick
   tick                     <-- subshell keeps ticking; SIGTERM ignored
   tick                     <-- wait hangs because process never exits
   ...
   ```

   Fix (either is sufficient):
   - **(a)** Drop `trap '' TERM` from the heartbeat subshell. The comment cites `set -e`/`set -o pipefail` protection but neither interacts with TERM traps — the trap as written has no protective effect against shell-error propagation. The natural failure mode of an `emit_heartbeat` error is a non-zero exit, not a TERM signal, so the trap is unnecessary.
   - **(b)** Change `stop_background_heartbeat` to use a signal the subshell does not trap:
     ```bash
     kill -INT "$HB_BG_PID" 2>/dev/null || true   # or -HUP, or -KILL
     ```

   Prefer (a) — it removes the dead defensive trap and lets the standard `kill && wait` idiom work as expected.

### Non-blocking

- **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1038-1050** — `check_idle_budget` double-alerts when the loop jumps straight from idle=0 to idle ≥ 2x budget (e.g. wrapper paused for 60+ min). The 2x branch fires (sets `ALERTED_AT_DOUBLE=true`) but `ALERTED_AT_BUDGET` stays false, so the next check fires the 1x alert too. Minor — recommend setting `ALERTED_AT_BUDGET=true` inside the 2x branch so the 1x is logically skipped (already covered by the 2x).

- **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1029-1035** — Idle-alert `--detail` carries role/slice/idle/budget but does not include the structured BRC state. Plan TASK-2-3 acceptance line 866-867: "alert payload includes anomaly type, priority, current BRC state". The current detail is human-readable but a stuck-phase-transition alert that does not surface the consensus matrix snapshot makes the operator pull state separately. Recommend embedding a compact `consensus.agents.{role}.{producer_phase,reviewer_phase,confirmed}` snippet in the detail.

- **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:928-937** — `fetch_next_action` swallows the 409 distinction by falling back to `{"action":"wait"}` on any non-zero CLI exit. The plan TASK-2-1 acceptance is satisfied (no retry-with-backoff; the next loop iteration re-fetches state via `brc get-state`), but the behavior is implicit. Recommend a `cw_log` warning when the fallback fires so operators reading the wrapper logs can distinguish 409-stale_version from transport failures.

### Verification

All 111 other TASK-2-6 tests pass against the current proposal — the
heartbeat trap bug is the only blocker. The flag-off byte-for-byte
template snapshot still holds. The TASK-2-7 in-process BRC regression
suite (`integration_tests/regression/test_brc_*.py`) runs green with
flag off.

      \ protection but neither interacts with TERM traps \u2014 the trap as written\
      \ has no protective effect against shell-error propagation. The natural failure\
      \ mode of an `emit_heartbeat` error is a non-zero exit, not a TERM signal, so\
      \ the trap is unnecessary.\n   - **(b)** Change `stop_background_heartbeat`\
      \ to use a signal the subshell does not trap:\n     ```bash\n     kill -INT\
      \ \"$HB_BG_PID\" 2>/dev/null || true   # or -HUP, or -KILL\n     ```\n\n   Prefer\
      \ (a) \u2014 it removes the dead defensive trap and lets the standard `kill\
      \ && wait` idiom work as expected.\n\n### Non-blocking\n\n- **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1038-1050**\
      \ \u2014 `check_idle_budget` double-alerts when the loop jumps straight from\
      \ idle=0 to idle \u2265 2x budget (e.g. wrapper paused for 60+ min). The 2x\
      \ branch fires (sets `ALERTED_AT_DOUBLE=true`) but `ALERTED_AT_BUDGET` stays\
      \ false, so the next check fires the 1x alert too. Minor \u2014 recommend setting\
      \ `ALERTED_AT_BUDGET=true` inside the 2x branch so the 1x is logically skipped\
      \ (already covered by the 2x).\n\n- **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1029-1035**\
      \ \u2014 Idle-alert `--detail` carries role/slice/idle/budget but does not include\
      \ the structured BRC state. Plan TASK-2-3 acceptance line 866-867: \"alert payload\
      \ includes anomaly type, priority, current BRC state\". The current detail is\
      \ human-readable but a stuck-phase-transition alert that does not surface the\
      \ consensus matrix snapshot makes the operator pull state separately. Recommend\
      \ embedding a compact `consensus.agents.{role}.{producer_phase,reviewer_phase,confirmed}`\
      \ snippet in the detail.\n\n- **orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:928-937**\
      \ \u2014 `fetch_next_action` swallows the 409 distinction by falling back to\
      \ `{\"action\":\"wait\"}` on any non-zero CLI exit. The plan TASK-2-1 acceptance\
      \ is satisfied (no retry-with-backoff; the next loop iteration re-fetches state\
      \ via `brc get-state`), but the behavior is implicit. Recommend a `cw_log` warning\
      \ when the fallback fires so operators reading the wrapper logs can distinguish\
      \ 409-stale_version from transport failures.\n\n### Verification\n\nAll 111\
      \ other TASK-2-6 tests pass against the current proposal \u2014 the\nheartbeat\
      \ trap bug is the only blocker. The flag-off byte-for-byte\ntemplate snapshot\
      \ still holds. The TASK-2-7 in-process BRC regression\nsuite (`integration_tests/regression/test_brc_*.py`)\
      \ runs green with\nflag off.\n"

    - orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py
    nack_version: 1

    \ trap; the subshell loops forever and the subsequent `wait $HB_BG_PID` blocks\
    \ indefinitely. **First call to `wait_for_event` returns \u2192 wrapper hangs\
    \ forever in `stop_background_heartbeat`.** The deterministic event-pump loop\
    \ wedges after one iteration \u2014 exactly the failure mode #2906 was meant to\
    \ eliminate.\n\n   Failing regression test:\n   ```\n   orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py::TestEventPumpHeartbeatSubshellLifecycle::test_flag_on_heartbeat_subshell_can_be_stopped\n\
    \   ```\n\n   Bash harness reproducing the lifecycle bug (verified locally):\n\
    \   ```\n   $ bash -c '( trap \"\" TERM; while true; do sleep 1; echo tick; done\
    \ ) &\n               sleep 2; kill $!; wait $! 2>/dev/null'\n   tick\n   tick\n\
    \   tick                     <-- subshell keeps ticking; SIGTERM ignored\n   tick\
    \                     <-- wait hangs because process never exits\n   ...\n   ```\n\
    \n   Fix (either is sufficient):\n   - **(a)** Drop `trap '' TERM` from the heartbeat\
    \ subshell. The comment cites `set -e`/`set -o pipefail` protection but neither\
    \ interacts with TERM traps \u2014 the trap as written has no protective effect\
    \ against shell-error propagation. The natural failure mode of an `emit_heartbeat`\

    \  - **(b)** Change `stop_background_heartbeat` to use a signal the subshell does\
    \ not trap:\n     ```bash\n     kill -INT \"$HB_BG_PID\" 2>/dev/null || true \
    \  # or -HUP, or -KILL\n     ```\n\n   Prefer (a) \u2014 it removes the dead defensive\
    \ trap and lets the standard `kill && wait` idiom work as expected.\n\n### Non-blocking\n\

### [2026-06-01T21:07:24Z] reviewer_code → coder (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_code for coder

Reviewed coder v1 (commit bca5c1ff0) — single-file diff adding `_EVENT_PUMP_WRAPPER_TEMPLATE`, helpers, and `build_event_pump_wrapped_command` to `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py`. The flag-off path is correctly preserved byte-for-byte (verified by inspection: the existing `_CONSENSUS_WRAPPER_TEMPLATE` and `build_consensus_wrapped_command` body are unmodified; new code is appended). The pre-confirm filter set, conditional `CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED` inclusion, slice_id propagation via env auto-attachment (`_maybe_attach_slice_id` in `_gateway.py:116`), 30-min idle budget default, idle alert anomaly/priority, and the `consensus confirmed` / `progress complete` pseudocode-typo guard all match the task-2-1..2-4 spec.

Two blocking correctness bugs in the event-pump template, both reproduced locally:

### Blocking

1. **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1052-1056` + `:1086-1092` — `note_progress` is called on the `wait` action regardless of whether the wait-loop actually matched, so the idle/no-progress safety budget (task-2-3) is non-functional in production.** The wait branch is:
   ```bash
   wait)
       wait_for_event "$ROLE_CONFIRMED" || true
       # Wait-loop returning (matched, timeout, or transient) is
       # progress in the event-pump sense: state may have changed.
       note_progress
       ;;
   ```
   `wait_for_event` invokes `egg-orch message wait-loop "${WAIT_ARGS[@]}" --max-iterations 1 >/dev/null 2>&1`. With `--max-iterations 1`, the wait-loop returns after a single inner wait — exit 0 on both **matched** AND **capped-without-match** (timeout), and stdout is discarded so the wrapper has no way to distinguish them. `note_progress` then unconditionally resets `LAST_PROGRESS=$SECONDS`. Result: in a genuinely quiet phase (no events for 30 min — the exact scenario `EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN` is designed to catch), the wait-loop returns every ~60 s (the inner timeout), `note_progress` resets the counter every ~60 s, the idle counter NEVER accumulates past one wait window, and `check_idle_budget` NEVER fires the `stuck-phase-transition` overseer alert. The legacy `MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS = 3` cap was load-bearing for this signal; replacing it with a non-functional idle budget is a silent regression of the contract task-2-3 acceptance ("idle budget threshold triggers overseer alert at configured duration"). The comment "state may have changed" misreads the contract — what matters is whether an *actionable BRC event* fired, not whether 60 s of wall-clock passed.

   **Fix options (pick one):**
   - Drop `note_progress` from the `wait` branch entirely; let the `confirm` / `propose|ack|nack` branches (which fire only on real BRC progress via next-action) be the sole resets. The idle counter then advances correctly during quiet stretches and resets when the agent is actually invoked.
   - Switch `wait_for_event` to `egg-orch message wait-loop --json ... | tee /tmp/wait-result.json` and only `note_progress` when `jq -r .matched` is `true`. Costlier but preserves the wait-branch reset on real matches.
   - Have next-action drive the reset: on next iteration, if action ≠ `wait`, that's the progress signal — call `note_progress` from inside the `confirm` / `propose|ack|nack` branches only (which is approximately what option 1 reduces to).

2. **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:852-870` — the background heartbeat subshell ignores SIGTERM, so `stop_background_heartbeat` can never kill it; the wrapper hangs in `wait $HB_BG_PID` on every shutdown until kubelet's grace-period SIGKILL fires.** The subshell is started with:
   ```bash
   (
       trap '' TERM
       while true; do
           sleep "$HB_INTERVAL_SECS"
           emit_heartbeat "WAITING_FOR_EVENT" "$body_text"
       done
   ) &
   ```
   The `trap '' TERM` IGNORES SIGTERM. Then `stop_background_heartbeat` does:
   ```bash
   kill "$HB_BG_PID" 2>/dev/null || true
   wait "$HB_BG_PID" 2>/dev/null || true
   ```
   `kill $PID` (no signal arg) sends SIGTERM by default — which the subshell now traps to nothing — so the subshell does NOT exit. `wait $HB_BG_PID` then blocks until the subshell terminates of its own accord (which it never does — `while true`). Verified locally with `kill -0` probe: the subshell remains alive after `kill $PID` and only dies on `kill -9`. The user-visible impact is that every cleanup path — `EXIT` trap on normal `exit 0` from the main loop AND `TERM` trap when the orchestrator sends SIGTERM — hangs in `wait` until kubelet's grace period (~30 s default) expires and SIGKILL tears down the process tree. The wait-loop also runs the same path on every iteration of the main loop (start + stop), but that one's safe because the subshell isn't blocking on TERM — the `kill` call is what fails. The rationale comment ("Detach from job control so the parent set -e / set -o pipefail doesn't kill the heartbeat subshell on a transient CLI error") is inaccurate: the template uses `set -uo pipefail`, NOT `set -e`, and `pipefail` doesn't propagate into background subshells anyway. The trap solves a non-problem and creates a real one.

   **Fix options (pick one):**
   - `trap 'exit 0' TERM` in the subshell — TERM cleanly exits the heartbeat loop, parent's `kill` + `wait` then complete in <1 s.
   - Drop the subshell `trap '' TERM` entirely — the parent's default kill-on-exit semantics will do the right thing.
   - Send a stronger signal: `kill -KILL "$HB_BG_PID"` in `stop_background_heartbeat`. Cruder than the trap fix but unblockable.

   Either fix needs the test in TASK-2-6 to be extended with a cleanup-cadence assertion (e.g. measured time from `stop_background_heartbeat` call to `wait` return — should be sub-second, not 30 s).

### Non-blocking

- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:928-937`** — `fetch_next_action` on any CLI error (including HTTP 409 `stale_version` / aggregated-NACK) falls back to `{"action":"wait"}`. The task-2-1 acceptance language is "re-fetch state without backoff"; the implementation does re-fetch on the next iteration but only after a 60 s wait-loop window. In practice the producer's re-propose will land as a `CONSENSUS_PROPOSE` and unblock the wait quickly, so the delay is bounded — but the implementation does NOT distinguish 409 from a generic transport failure, both 100 % swallowed by `2>/dev/null`. A more faithful reading of the spec would `continue` straight to the next loop iteration on 409 (no wait, no `note_progress`), preserving the idle counter and re-fetching state immediately. Worth at least logging the 409 to stderr so an operator can see which code path triggered.
- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:837-845`** — the `--body "$body_text (slice=$slice_tag)"` suffix with `slice_tag="${EGG_SLICE_ID:-none}"` echoes the literal string "slice=none" into the body when `EGG_SLICE_ID` is unset (pipeline-level spawns). The *structured* `slice_id` field is correctly handled by `_maybe_attach_slice_id` (it omits the field rather than emitting `""`), but the body-text suffix creates an independent grep target. The task-2-6 (iii) acceptance specifies "the heartbeat payload includes slice_id sourced from EGG_SLICE_ID (verified by asserting the request body in a mock unit test)" — if the test asserts on the structured `slice_id` JSON field, the body-suffix is harmless redundancy; if the test asserts on the body string via `(slice=...)` grep, it bypasses the actual `_maybe_attach_slice_id` wiring and could pass even if the structured slice_id regressed. Either tighten task-2-6's assertion to the structured field, or drop the body-suffix (since `_maybe_attach_slice_id` already handles it correctly).
- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1136-1153`** — `build_event_pump_wrapped_command(prompt_text, ...)` accepts `prompt_text` and immediately `del`s it, with a docstring noting slice-3 will wire it through `compose_event_prompt`. Interface parity is fine, but a future caller passing a meaningful prompt would get silent discard. Worth at least asserting the discard intent (e.g. a debug `cw_log` echoing the bootstrap prompt length) so a slice-3 wiring regression doesn't go unnoticed.
- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1025-1036`** — the idle-budget alert priority is hard-coded to `"high"` at both threshold and 2× boundary. The contract task-2-3 text says "raise the alert priority", but the overseer alert priority enum caps at `"high"` (no `"critical"` level), so there is no level higher to escalate to. The implementation distinguishes the two alerts via summary text suffix `" (2x budget)"` which is a reasonable adaptation, but a slice-2 docs follow-up should note that "escalation" at this boundary is by-summary-text (and frequency: a second alert fires), not by-priority-level.

Re-propose path: rebuild on top of the same slice-2 base; tests for the idle-budget regression and the cleanup-cadence regression both want to run under `make test` before re-proposing.

      \ The flag-off path is correctly preserved byte-for-byte (verified by inspection:\
      \ the existing `_CONSENSUS_WRAPPER_TEMPLATE` and `build_consensus_wrapped_command`\
      \ body are unmodified; new code is appended). The pre-confirm filter set, conditional\
      \ `CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED` inclusion, slice_id propagation via env auto-attachment\
      \ (`_maybe_attach_slice_id` in `_gateway.py:116`), 30-min idle budget default,\
      \ idle alert anomaly/priority, and the `consensus confirmed` / `progress complete`\
      \ pseudocode-typo guard all match the task-2-1..2-4 spec.\n\nTwo blocking correctness\
      \ bugs in the event-pump template, both reproduced locally:\n\n### Blocking\n\
      \n1. **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1052-1056` + `:1086-1092` \u2014 `note_progress`\
      \ is called on the `wait` action regardless of whether the wait-loop actually\
      \ matched, so the idle/no-progress safety budget (task-2-3) is non-functional\
      \ in production.** The wait branch is:\n   ```bash\n   wait)\n       wait_for_event\
      \ \"$ROLE_CONFIRMED\" || true\n       # Wait-loop returning (matched, timeout,\
      \ or transient) is\n       # progress in the event-pump sense: state may have\
      \ changed.\n       note_progress\n       ;;\n   ```\n   `wait_for_event` invokes\
      \ `egg-orch message wait-loop \"${WAIT_ARGS[@]}\" --max-iterations 1 >/dev/null\
      \ 2>&1`. With `--max-iterations 1`, the wait-loop returns after a single inner\
      \ wait \u2014 exit 0 on both **matched** AND **capped-without-match** (timeout),\
      \ and stdout is discarded so the wrapper has no way to distinguish them. `note_progress`\
      \ then unconditionally resets `LAST_PROGRESS=$SECONDS`. Result: in a genuinely\
      \ quiet phase (no events for 30 min \u2014 the exact scenario `EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN`\
      \ is designed to catch), the wait-loop returns every ~60 s (the inner timeout),\
      \ `note_progress` resets the counter every ~60 s, the idle counter NEVER accumulates\
      \ past one wait window, and `check_idle_budget` NEVER fires the `stuck-phase-transition`\
      \ overseer alert. The legacy `MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS = 3` cap was load-bearing\
      \ for this signal; replacing it with a non-functional idle budget is a silent\
      \ regression of the contract task-2-3 acceptance (\"idle budget threshold triggers\
      \ overseer alert at configured duration\"). The comment \"state may have changed\"\
      \ misreads the contract \u2014 what matters is whether an *actionable BRC event*\
      \ fired, not whether 60 s of wall-clock passed.\n\n   **Fix options (pick one):**\n\
      \   - Drop `note_progress` from the `wait` branch entirely; let the `confirm`\
      \ / `propose|ack|nack` branches (which fire only on real BRC progress via next-action)\
      \ be the sole resets. The idle counter then advances correctly during quiet\
      \ stretches and resets when the agent is actually invoked.\n   - Switch `wait_for_event`\
      \ to `egg-orch message wait-loop --json ... | tee /tmp/wait-result.json` and\
      \ only `note_progress` when `jq -r .matched` is `true`. Costlier but preserves\
      \ the wait-branch reset on real matches.\n   - Have next-action drive the reset:\
      \ on next iteration, if action \u2260 `wait`, that's the progress signal \u2014\
      \ call `note_progress` from inside the `confirm` / `propose|ack|nack` branches\
      \ only (which is approximately what option 1 reduces to).\n\n2. **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:852-870`\
      \ \u2014 the background heartbeat subshell ignores SIGTERM, so `stop_background_heartbeat`\
      \ can never kill it; the wrapper hangs in `wait $HB_BG_PID` on every shutdown\
      \ until kubelet's grace-period SIGKILL fires.** The subshell is started with:\n\
      \   ```bash\n   (\n       trap '' TERM\n       while true; do\n           sleep\
      \ \"$HB_INTERVAL_SECS\"\n           emit_heartbeat \"WAITING_FOR_EVENT\" \"\
      $body_text\"\n       done\n   ) &\n   ```\n   The `trap '' TERM` IGNORES SIGTERM.\
      \ Then `stop_background_heartbeat` does:\n   ```bash\n   kill \"$HB_BG_PID\"\
      \ 2>/dev/null || true\n   wait \"$HB_BG_PID\" 2>/dev/null || true\n   ```\n\
      \   `kill $PID` (no signal arg) sends SIGTERM by default \u2014 which the subshell\
      \ now traps to nothing \u2014 so the subshell does NOT exit. `wait $HB_BG_PID`\
      \ then blocks until the subshell terminates of its own accord (which it never\
      \ does \u2014 `while true`). Verified locally with `kill -0` probe: the subshell\
      \ remains alive after `kill $PID` and only dies on `kill -9`. The user-visible\
      \ impact is that every cleanup path \u2014 `EXIT` trap on normal `exit 0` from\
      \ the main loop AND `TERM` trap when the orchestrator sends SIGTERM \u2014 hangs\
      \ in `wait` until kubelet's grace period (~30 s default) expires and SIGKILL\
      \ tears down the process tree. The wait-loop also runs the same path on every\
      \ iteration of the main loop (start + stop), but that one's safe because the\
      \ subshell isn't blocking on TERM \u2014 the `kill` call is what fails. The\
      \ rationale comment (\"Detach from job control so the parent set -e / set -o\
      \ pipefail doesn't kill the heartbeat subshell on a transient CLI error\") is\
      \ inaccurate: the template uses `set -uo pipefail`, NOT `set -e`, and `pipefail`\
      \ doesn't propagate into background subshells anyway. The trap solves a non-problem\
      \ and creates a real one.\n\n   **Fix options (pick one):**\n   - `trap 'exit\
      \ 0' TERM` in the subshell \u2014 TERM cleanly exits the heartbeat loop, parent's\
      \ `kill` + `wait` then complete in <1 s.\n   - Drop the subshell `trap '' TERM`\
      \ entirely \u2014 the parent's default kill-on-exit semantics will do the right\
      \ thing.\n   - Send a stronger signal: `kill -KILL \"$HB_BG_PID\"` in `stop_background_heartbeat`.\
      \ Cruder than the trap fix but unblockable.\n\n   Either fix needs the test\
      \ in TASK-2-6 to be extended with a cleanup-cadence assertion (e.g. measured\
      \ time from `stop_background_heartbeat` call to `wait` return \u2014 should\
      \ be sub-second, not 30 s).\n\n### Non-blocking\n\n- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:928-937`**\
      \ \u2014 `fetch_next_action` on any CLI error (including HTTP 409 `stale_version`\
      \ / aggregated-NACK) falls back to `{\"action\":\"wait\"}`. The task-2-1 acceptance\
      \ language is \"re-fetch state without backoff\"; the implementation does re-fetch\
      \ on the next iteration but only after a 60 s wait-loop window. In practice\
      \ the producer's re-propose will land as a `CONSENSUS_PROPOSE` and unblock the\
      \ wait quickly, so the delay is bounded \u2014 but the implementation does NOT\
      \ distinguish 409 from a generic transport failure, both 100 % swallowed by\
      \ `2>/dev/null`. A more faithful reading of the spec would `continue` straight\
      \ to the next loop iteration on 409 (no wait, no `note_progress`), preserving\
      \ the idle counter and re-fetching state immediately. Worth at least logging\
      \ the 409 to stderr so an operator can see which code path triggered.\n- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:837-845`**\
      \ \u2014 the `--body \"$body_text (slice=$slice_tag)\"` suffix with `slice_tag=\"\
      ${EGG_SLICE_ID:-none}\"` echoes the literal string \"slice=none\" into the body\

      \ and immediately `del`s it, with a docstring noting slice-3 will wire it through\
      \ `compose_event_prompt`. Interface parity is fine, but a future caller passing\
      \ a meaningful prompt would get silent discard. Worth at least asserting the\
      \ discard intent (e.g. a debug `cw_log` echoing the bootstrap prompt length)\

   ```bash
   wait)
       wait_for_event "$ROLE_CONFIRMED" || true
       # Wait-loop returning (matched, timeout, or transient) is
       # progress in the event-pump sense: state may have changed.
       note_progress
       ;;
   ```
   `wait_for_event` invokes `egg-orch message wait-loop "${WAIT_ARGS[@]}" --max-iterations 1 >/dev/null 2>&1`. With `--max-iterations 1`, the wait-loop returns after a single inner wait — exit 0 on both **matched** AND **capped-without-match** (timeout), and stdout is discarded so the wrapper has no way to distinguish them. `note_progress` then unconditionally resets `LAST_PROGRESS=$SECONDS`. Result: in a genuinely quiet phase (no events for 30 min — the exact scenario `EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN` is designed to catch), the wait-loop returns every ~60 s (the inner timeout), `note_progress` resets the counter every ~60 s, the idle counter NEVER accumulates past one wait window, and `check_idle_budget` NEVER fires the `stuck-phase-transition` overseer alert. The legacy `MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS = 3` cap was load-bearing for this signal; replacing it with a non-functional idle budget is a silent regression of the contract task-2-3 acceptance ("idle budget threshold triggers overseer alert at configured duration"). The comment "state may have changed" misreads the contract — what matters is whether an *actionable BRC event* fired, not whether 60 s of wall-clock passed. **Fix options:** (a) drop `note_progress` from the `wait` branch entirely — let `confirm`/`propose|ack|nack` (real BRC progress via next-action) be the sole resets; (b) switch `wait_for_event` to `--json` and only `note_progress` when `.matched == true`; (c) have next-action drive the reset — only call `note_progress` from the actionable branches.

2. **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:850-870` — the background heartbeat subshell ignores SIGTERM, so `stop_background_heartbeat` can never kill it; the wrapper hangs in `wait $HB_BG_PID` on every shutdown until kubelet's grace-period SIGKILL fires.** The subshell is started with `trap '' TERM` (ignores SIGTERM), then `stop_background_heartbeat` does `kill "$HB_BG_PID"` (default signal = SIGTERM, ignored) followed by `wait "$HB_BG_PID"` (blocks). Verified locally with `kill -0` probe: the subshell remains alive after `kill $PID` and only dies on `kill -9`. The user-visible impact: every cleanup path — `EXIT` trap on normal `exit 0` AND `TERM` trap on orchestrator SIGTERM — hangs in `wait` until kubelet's grace period (~30 s default) expires and SIGKILL tears down the process tree. The rationale comment ("Detach from job control so the parent set -e / set -o pipefail doesn't kill us") is inaccurate — the template uses `set -uo pipefail`, NOT `set -e`, and `pipefail` doesn't propagate into background subshells anyway. The trap solves a non-problem and creates a real one. **Fix options:** (a) `trap 'exit 0' TERM` in the subshell — TERM cleanly exits the heartbeat loop; (b) drop the subshell `trap '' TERM` entirely — parent's kill-on-exit semantics already do the right thing; (c) `kill -KILL "$HB_BG_PID"` in `stop_background_heartbeat`. Whichever fix lands, task-2-6 should be extended with a cleanup-cadence assertion (measured time from `stop_background_heartbeat` call to `wait` return — should be sub-second, not 30 s).

### Non-blocking

- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:928-937`** — `fetch_next_action` on any CLI error (including HTTP 409 `stale_version` / aggregated-NACK) falls back to `{"action":"wait"}` and swallows stderr via `2>/dev/null`. Task-2-1 acceptance language is "re-fetch state without backoff"; the implementation does re-fetch on the next iteration but only after a 60 s wait-loop window. A more faithful reading would `continue` straight to the next loop iteration on 409 (no wait, no `note_progress`), preserving the idle counter and re-fetching state immediately. Worth logging the 409 to stderr so an operator can see which code path triggered.
- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:837-845`** — the `--body "$body_text (slice=$slice_tag)"` suffix with `slice_tag="${EGG_SLICE_ID:-none}"` echoes the literal string "slice=none" into the body when `EGG_SLICE_ID` is unset. The structured `slice_id` field is correctly handled by `_maybe_attach_slice_id` (omits rather than emitting `""`), but the body-text suffix creates an independent grep target. If task-2-6 (iii) tests assert on the structured JSON field, the body-suffix is harmless redundancy; if it greps the body string, it bypasses the real wiring and could pass even if structured slice_id regressed. Either tighten task-2-6's assertion to the structured field, or drop the body-suffix entirely.
- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1136-1153`** — `build_event_pump_wrapped_command(prompt_text, ...)` accepts `prompt_text` and immediately `del`s it. Interface parity is fine, but a future caller passing a meaningful prompt would get silent discard. Worth at least a debug `cw_log` echoing the bootstrap prompt length so a slice-3 wiring regression doesn't go unnoticed.
- **`orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:1025-1049`** — the idle-budget alert priority is hard-coded to `"high"` at both threshold and 2× boundary, distinguished only by summary-text suffix `" (2x budget)"`. The contract says "raise the alert priority", but the overseer alert priority enum caps at `"high"`. Adaptation by-summary-text is reasonable but worth noting in the slice-2 docs follow-up (the wait-pattern doc currently says "the alert priority escalates" which a reader would interpret as a level rise).

Re-propose path: rebuild on top of the same slice-2 base; tests for the idle-budget regression and the cleanup-cadence regression both want to run under `make test` before re-proposing.

### [2026-06-01T21:09:00Z] coder (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from coder

[#2908 slice-2 coder v2] Re-propose addressing all 3 NACKing reviewers' blocking findings (open-NACK barrier was acknowledged in prior call; this is the post-aggregation retry).

v1 NACKs from reviewer_code, reviewer_concurrency, tester all cite the same two blocking findings; both fixed in this re-propose (commit fec35645d):

**Blocking #1 (all 3 NACKs): Heartbeat subshell deadlock.** `trap '' TERM` masked SIGTERM that `stop_background_heartbeat` sends; `wait $HB_BG_PID` blocked forever, deadlocking the entire event-pump after the first wait-loop return. Replaced with `trap 'exit 0' TERM` (the fix all 3 reviewers proposed). Standalone bash reproducer of the lifecycle (start -> blocking wait -> stop) now completes in ~100 ms instead of hanging. Comment about `set -e`/`pipefail` rewritten per reviewer observations.

**Blocking #2 (reviewer_code, reviewer_concurrency): Idle budget defeated.** Unconditional `note_progress` on every `wait` return reset the idle counter every ~60 s; the 30-min budget could never expire. Now captures `wait_rc` and only calls `note_progress` when rc==0 (matched event). Exact patch reviewer_concurrency proposed. Timeouts now correctly age the idle counter.

**Non-blocking polish (tester #1/#2/#3 + reviewer_code #1):**
- `check_idle_budget` 2x branch sets `ALERTED_AT_BUDGET=true` so 1x alert isn't re-fired after the loop jumps idle=0 straight to 2x (tester #1).
- Idle alert detail now embeds `consensus.agents.<role>` + `blocking_agents` snapshot from STATE_JSON; degrades to "(unavailable)" on malformed JSON. Plan TASK-2-3 acceptance "alert payload includes ... current BRC state" (tester #2).
- `fetch_next_action` now `cw_log`s rc+reason when falling back to `{"action":"wait"}` so operators can distinguish 409 (expected event-pump signal) from transport failures (tester #3, reviewer_code #1).

**Deferred observations (not blocking):**
- reviewer_code #2 (body-suffix `(slice=...)` as independent grep target): structured `slice_id` is auto-attached by `_maybe_attach_slice_id` and is the assertion source for task-2-6; body-suffix is harmless redundancy for human grep.
- reviewer_code #3 (`build_event_pump_wrapped_command(prompt_text)` discard): explicit `del` plus docstring make slice-3 wiring intent unambiguous.
- reviewer_code #4 (`high` priority at both 1x and 2x): overseer alert enum caps at "high"; escalation is by summary text + alert frequency, which is a slice-2 docs concern.

Verification: 83 wrapper tests pass; 174 extended tests pass; ruff/format/mypy clean; flag-on `bash -n` clean; flag-off rendered bash byte-for-byte identical to baseline.

**Adversarial re-review**

**Your v2 review has TWO equal-weight mandates:**

1. **Verify named v1 blockers were addressed** — confirm the producer fixed what you NACK'd.
2. **Audit the delta since your last review (per REVIEWER-SYNC.md: `git log {last_reviewed_commit}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` — the commits landed since the version you last verdicted, NOT the whole accumulated proposal surface) as a fresh reviewer** — ignore your v1 NACK history. Read that diff as if you'd never seen the prior version. Apply your lens (security threat-model, concurrency races, contract AC, line-by-line bugs, silent-fallback shapes — whichever your role owns) to the delta itself, not to whether your previous concerns were satisfied. **Mandate 2 is bounded to this delta** — it does NOT ask you to re-traverse the whole accumulated surface from earlier cycles; that work was amortized when you first reviewed those commits.

Both mandates have equal weight. If (1) passes but (2) finds new issues, you NACK. ACK requires both pass.

**The named-blockers anchor is a known trap. Every reviewer lens has a mandate-2 in its own territory** — security has newly-introduced threat surfaces, concurrency has newly-introduced races, contract has newly-introduced AC drift, code has newly-introduced line-by-line bugs. The four issues that escaped PR #2724 to the GitHub bot were all of code-lens shape (`${ANSWER}` as bare Python, deprecated `datetime.utcnow()`, non-atomic write, bare `except: pass`) — the persistent reviewer correctly answered mandate 1 ("did prior issues get fixed? yes") and skipped mandate 2 ("does this delta introduce new issues? actually yes"). The shape generalizes: whatever your lens, this delta can introduce issues your prior NACK didn't name. Watching the producer deliver a targeted fix pulls strongly toward "verify my fix-request landed → ACK." Recognize the pull and do mandate 2 anyway.

**How to execute mandate 2:**

- Read each new hunk as an operator who's about to copy-paste / run / integrate it. Would this code execute as written? Would these docs send a copy-paster down a working path?
- Apply every rubric pass to the new hunks. New issues outside the scope of your prior NACK are blocking; your prior NACK does not bound this re-review.
- **Fresh-reviewer simulation.** Before issuing your v2 verdict, ask: would a reviewer who has only seen this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK history ACK this? If you can't argue yes from that diff alone, NACK.
- **External-bot anchor.** Imagine `egg-reviewer[bot]` reads only this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK context. What would it flag? Anything it'd flag, you should NACK first.

**Your v2 verdict must enumerate both halves** so mandate 2 doesn't silently disappear from the record:

- (a) Which v1 blockers you verified-fixed (mandate 1).
- (b) What new issues you audited-and-did-not-find (mandate 2). Name the specific shapes you checked — not "reviewed thoroughly," but "checked for silent fallbacks, doc-snippet executability, API-deprecation, atomicity of file writes." If you can't enumerate (b), you haven't done mandate 2.

**Re-reviews are cheap by design.** Your amortized context means the work is "read the delta, apply your rubric, decide" — minutes, not hours. NACK without hesitance; the orchestrator absorbs cycles. Two NACKs on the same producer where the second names new findings is the correct trajectory, not goalpost-moving. The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta. Anything it catches that lives in this cycle's diff is a miss attributable to this re-review.

````yaml
id: c2a9ccad-f6cc-42
phase: implement
metadata:
  payload:
    summary: '[#2908 slice-2 coder v2] Re-propose addressing all 3 NACKing reviewers''
      blocking findings (open-NACK barrier was acknowledged in prior call; this is
      the post-aggregation retry).


      v1 NACKs from reviewer_code, reviewer_concurrency, tester all cite the same
      two blocking findings; both fixed in this re-propose (commit fec35645d):


      **Blocking #1 (all 3 NACKs): Heartbeat subshell deadlock.** `trap '''' TERM`
      masked SIGTERM that `stop_background_heartbeat` sends; `wait $HB_BG_PID` blocked
      forever, deadlocking the entire event-pump after the first wait-loop return.
      Replaced with `trap ''exit 0'' TERM` (the fix all 3 reviewers proposed). Standalone
      bash reproducer of the lifecycle (start -> blocking wait -> stop) now completes
      in ~100 ms instead of hanging. Comment about `set -e`/`pipefail` rewritten per
      reviewer observations.


      **Blocking #2 (reviewer_code, reviewer_concurrency): Idle budget defeated.**
      Unconditional `note_progress` on every `wait` return reset the idle counter
      every ~60 s; the 30-min budget could never expire. Now captures `wait_rc` and
      only calls `note_progress` when rc==0 (matched event). Exact patch reviewer_concurrency
      proposed. Timeouts now correctly age the idle counter.


      **Non-blocking polish (tester #1/#2/#3 + reviewer_code #1):**

      - `check_idle_budget` 2x branch sets `ALERTED_AT_BUDGET=true` so 1x alert isn''t
      re-fired after the loop jumps idle=0 straight to 2x (tester #1).

      - Idle alert detail now embeds `consensus.agents.<role>` + `blocking_agents`
      snapshot from STATE_JSON; degrades to "(unavailable)" on malformed JSON. Plan
      TASK-2-3 acceptance "alert payload includes ... current BRC state" (tester #2).

      - `fetch_next_action` now `cw_log`s rc+reason when falling back to `{"action":"wait"}`
      so operators can distinguish 409 (expected event-pump signal) from transport
      failures (tester #3, reviewer_code #1).


      **Deferred observations (not blocking):**

      - reviewer_code #2 (body-suffix `(slice=...)` as independent grep target): structured
      `slice_id` is auto-attached by `_maybe_attach_slice_id` and is the assertion
      source for task-2-6; body-suffix is harmless redundancy for human grep.

      - reviewer_code #3 (`build_event_pump_wrapped_command(prompt_text)` discard):
      explicit `del` plus docstring make slice-3 wiring intent unambiguous.

      - reviewer_code #4 (`high` priority at both 1x and 2x): overseer alert enum
      caps at "high"; escalation is by summary text + alert frequency, which is a
      slice-2 docs concern.


      Verification: 83 wrapper tests pass; 174 extended tests pass; ruff/format/mypy
      clean; flag-on `bash -n` clean; flag-off rendered bash byte-for-byte identical
      to baseline.'
    attestation: {}
    artifacts:
    - orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py
    risk_considered: All three NACKs are now addressed. Both blocking findings (heartbeat
      trap + idle-budget reset) are fixed with the exact patches the reviewers proposed
      and verified locally (standalone bash reproducer for the trap fix; the wait_rc
      gate is reviewer_concurrency's verbatim suggestion). Three non-blocking polish
      items also addressed without semantic change. Flag-off path remains byte-for-byte
      unchanged so reviewer_security and reviewer_contract's v1 ACKs still apply to
      the v2 superset.
    commit_sha: fec35645dd86d38a3d16159c11679aac78a496bf
    files_changed:
    - orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py
    tests_run: []
    tasks_satisfied:
    - task-2-1
    - task-2-2
    - task-2-3
    - task-2-4
  version: 2
  commit_sha: fec35645dd86d38a3d16159c11679aac78a496bf
  slice_id: slice-2
````

### [2026-06-01T21:10:35Z] reviewer_contract → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_contract for coder


Contract verification re-review (cycle 2) — proposal v2, head commit `fec35645d`, delta = `bca5c1ff0..fec35645d` (two commits, `f870b62d1` + `fec35645d`), still single-file scope on `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py`. My v1 verdict was an ACK, so mandate 1 is trivially satisfied — no v1 blockers I named to re-verify. Executed mandate 2 (fresh-reviewer audit of the v1→v2 delta through the contract lens) below.

### Mandate 1 — v1 blockers I named
I ACKed v1; I did not block on anything. Trivially satisfied. (Other reviewers' blockers — heartbeat trap deadlock, idle-budget defeat — are out of my lens and are visible in this delta's commit messages and code.)

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of the v1→v2 delta (contract lens)
Delta enumeration — six hunks:

1. **`start_background_heartbeat` trap rewrite** (`trap '' TERM` → `trap 'exit 0' TERM`) + comment overhaul. Pure concurrency-correctness fix; **no contract surface touched.** Heartbeat cadence, payload shape, and slice_id-propagation invariant (task-2-2) are unchanged — the trap fix only governs subshell teardown.

2. **`stop_background_heartbeat` comment-only**. No semantic change.

3. **`fetch_next_action` restructure** (capture rc, log fallback). Behavior preserved: on any non-zero CLI exit or empty body, the function still returns `{"action":"wait"}` so the next loop iteration re-derives state via `fetch_state`. The new `cw_log "brc next-action returned rc=$rc / empty body; falling back…"` is observability-only and does not alter control flow. **Task-2-1 acceptance "Wrapper handles 409 stale_version and 409 aggregated-NACK … as event-pump signals (re-fetch state, re-invoke), NOT as transient crashes to retry with backoff"** remains satisfied (still no backoff loop here; still re-derives via state on next iteration).

4. **`raise_idle_alert` adds `brc_snapshot` to `--detail`**. The snapshot extracts `consensus.agents.<role>.{producer_phase,reviewer_phase,confirmed}` + `blocking_agents` from `STATE_JSON` (already fetched in the main loop, so no extra network hop) and embeds it in the alert detail string. On malformed/missing STATE_JSON the python heredoc prints `(unavailable)`; on python-failure the outer `|| echo` prints `(snapshot unavailable)`. Both fallbacks degrade to string content inside the alert detail and do not affect alert priority, anomaly type, or whether the alert fires — so this is a strictly additive contract observation. Aligns with the plan-side TASK-2-3 alert-payload language (anomaly + priority + BRC state); task-2-3 as written in this contract doesn't strictly require it, but the addition is contract-consistent and not in tension.

5. **`check_idle_budget` 2x branch sets `ALERTED_AT_BUDGET=true`**. Closes a latch race where, if the loop jumped from idle=0 straight past `2 × IDLE_BUDGET_SECS` between checks, the 2x branch fired but `ALERTED_AT_BUDGET` stayed false, so a subsequent `check_idle_budget` would also fire the now-subsumed 1x alert. **Improves task-2-3 fidelity**: the contract says one alert at budget, escalate at 2x — emitting a redundant 1x alert after the 2x already fired would violate that intent. The latch fix is correct.

6. **`wait` case captures `wait_rc` and gates `note_progress` on rc==0**. This is the most important contract-relevant change. The v1 implementation unconditionally called `note_progress` after every `wait_for_event` return, including the per-iteration `--max-iterations 1 --timeout 60` timeout returns. Because the wait-loop returns rc=1 every ~60 s when nothing arrives, the idle counter was being reset every minute, so the 30-min `EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN` budget could never expire and the OVERSEER_ALERT safety net (task-2-3's whole reason for existing) could never fire. The new code only resets the counter on rc==0 (a real matched event). **Without this fix, task-2-3's acceptance criterion ("no actionable event for the budget duration → raise OVERSEER_ALERT") was unreachable in v1.** v2 makes the contract criterion actually achievable.

Specific contract-shape checks I ran on the delta (not "reviewed thoroughly"):
- **Silent fallbacks that mask contract regressions**: the new `(unavailable)` / `(snapshot unavailable)` strings in `raise_idle_alert` are visible debug-text inside the alert detail; they don't suppress the alert itself or change its routing. The `fetch_next_action` `cw_log` is purely additive observability. No new silent fallback that could swallow a contract failure.
- **Scope creep**: delta touches `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py` only — same file as v1, matches the proposal's `files_changed` and `artifacts` claims; no orphan changes.
- **Tasks-satisfied claim integrity**: v2 still claims only `task-2-1..task-2-4`; no expansion into 2-5/2-6/2-7 territory.
- **Old / flag-off template path**: the proposal claims "Flag-off rendered bash still byte-for-byte identical to baseline." The delta diff confirms all changes are inside `_EVENT_PUMP_WRAPPER_TEMPLATE` / event-pump helpers; the legacy template path is untouched. **Task-2-2 invariant "Keep the agent-side heartbeat path in the *old* template path verbatim" still holds.**
- **Task-2-3 priority-escalation wording note from v1**: still applies (`high` at both 1x and 2x because the OVERSEER_ALERT enum caps there). The delta did not address this — and shouldn't, since it's contract-wording ambiguity, not a producer bug. Same non-blocking observation; not re-raising.
- **Task-2-4 structural-shape note from v1**: still applies (keep-alive collapsed into the heartbeat subshell rather than a separate emitter). The delta does not regress this; functional equivalence preserved.

### Verdict
ACK. Mandate 1 trivially satisfied (no v1 blockers from me to verify). Mandate 2 contract-lens audit finds no new contract regressions in the v1→v2 delta; the `wait`-case rc gating actually makes task-2-3's idle-budget acceptance criterion reachable for the first time, and the other hunks are either correctness fixes or strictly additive enhancements. Same two v1 non-blocking notes (task-2-3 priority wording, task-2-4 collapsed keep-alive) remain observation-only.
