### [2026-06-01T21:31:46Z] reviewer_code_holistic → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code_holistic for coder


### Holistic-lens re-ACK — coder v3 (commit 2584da84d)

V3 delta vs my prior v2 ACK is a 13-line fix to `raise_idle_alert`'s `brc_snapshot` extraction. I missed this bug in my v2 review (Pass 4 silent-fallback hunt should have flagged `(snapshot unavailable)` as a silent fallback masking a misconfiguration); the tester caught it as a v2 NACK.

**The v2 bug, in shape:** the bash parameter expansion `${STATE_JSON:-{}}` is left-greedy on the closing brace — bash parses `${STATE_JSON:-{}` (default value: literal `{`) and appends the trailing `}` as plain text. Populated STATE_JSON values get an extra `}` glued on, the downstream `python3 -c "json.load(sys.stdin)"` raises `JSONDecodeError: Extra data`, the `|| echo "(snapshot unavailable)"` swallows it, and the alert detail always reads `(unavailable)` exactly when STATE_JSON IS populated (the common case during the event-pump loop). The empty / unset case happens to render as a usable `{}` by accident, so the bug is invisible in any test that doesn't exercise the populated-state path.

**Re-walked all four passes on v3 specifically (not the whole diff):**

- **Pass 1 (end-to-end primary use case):** Empirically reproduced the v2 corruption (`{"consensus":{...}}}` — three close-braces, json.load fails) and the v3 fix (`snapshot_input="${STATE_JSON-}"` + `[ -z … ] && snapshot_input='{}'` + `printf '%s' "$snapshot_input" | python3 …`) — populated STATE_JSON round-trips through json.load to the right dict; unset STATE_JSON gives `{}` → empty dict. The fix is correct.
- **Pass 2 (doc ↔ code symmetry):** orchestrator.md / agent-wait-patterns.md don't mention the snapshot-block shape, so the v3 diff doesn't disturb the doc surface. The only outstanding doc-vs-code symmetry observation is the "second background subshell" overclaim called out in my v2 ACK — non-blocking, unchanged in v3.
- **Pass 3 (synthetic-key / sentinel audit):** the BRC-snapshot consumer is the operator (read by humans through the overseer alert detail field). The keys it pulls — `consensus.agents.<role>.{producer_phase, reviewer_phase, confirmed}` + `consensus.blocking_agents` — are the same ones `tracker.evaluate()` produces (peer_consensus.py:1620-1656). Symmetric.
- **Pass 4 (silent-fallback hunt):** the `|| echo "(snapshot unavailable)"` fallback path is still present in v3 (defensible — JSON parse failure on a malformed state response shouldn't crash the alert). v3's fix moves the failure mode from "always silent under the common case" back to "silent only on genuinely malformed state". The fallback no longer masks an operator-facing misconfiguration in the success path.

Code is good. The flag-off path remains byte-for-byte unchanged.

### Non-blocking
- **My v2 review missed this.** Rendering the bash and grepping the source caught the trap-TERM hang (Pass 1) but I did not exec the rendered snapshot block against a populated STATE_JSON (Pass 1 end-to-end exec). Pass 1 end-to-end probing in a future cycle should include "render → exec a representative slice of each command-substitution block under a controlled harness", not just static read. The tester's adversarial coverage already pins this; lesson noted on the holistic side.
- Doc symmetry concern from my v2 ACK ("second background subshell") still applies but stays non-blocking.

### [2026-06-01T21:32:27Z] reviewer_code_holistic → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code_holistic for tester


### Holistic-lens ACK — tester v1 (commit b6d54431f, cherry-picked onto coder v3 tip 2584da84d)

Reviewed all four tester commits (94d942921 → 59f8b0f97 → 5fad02c50 → b6d54431f) against the four holistic passes plus contract acceptance for TASK-2-6 (i)..(ix) and TASK-2-7.

### Pass 1 — End-to-end primary use case (does the test suite drive the wrapper's primary use case?)
The 11 new test classes pin the wrapper as the operator-facing object — flag-on rendered bash is the system under test, not the Python composer:
- `TestEventPumpTemplateSelection` (i): flag-off byte-for-byte snapshot + flag-on emission + six-event wait-filter set.
- `TestEventPumpHeartbeatCadence` (ii)+(iii): background subshell, 30 s cadence, slice_id from EGG_SLICE_ID.
- `TestEventPumpKeepAliveCadence` (iv): wrapper-side keep-alive (verifying the slice_id-on-heartbeat propagation that triggers server-side `_refresh_gateway_session`).
- `TestEventPumpIdleBudgetAlert` (v): 30-min default, overseer-alert + continue-blocking, no FAILED transition.
- `TestEventPumpStaleVersionRefetch` (vi): 409 → wait re-fetch, NOT retry-with-backoff.
- `TestEventPumpRoleCompleteConfirm` (vii)+(vii.b): `consensus confirmed` on complete + no `progress complete` ghost call.
- `TestEventPumpWaitFilterConditional` (viii): CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED omitted pre-confirm, included post-confirm.
- `TestEventPumpSliceIdHeartbeatEdge` (ix): unset EGG_SLICE_ID emits null/omitted, NOT empty string.

All nine plan acceptance sub-criteria are pinned, and the two cross-cutting classes (`TestEventPumpFlagIsolation`, `TestEventPumpHeartbeatSubshellLifecycle`) guard against legacy-state bleed into the flag-on path.

### Pass 2 — Doc ↔ code symmetry
The `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py` docstring update transcribes the slice-2 verification stance from contract task-2-7 verbatim: flag-off regression only; no flag-on E2E in this slice because ScriptedProvider is ruled out per #2474; true E2E deferred to slice-4's `egg_stack` spike. This matches:
- `docs/architecture/orchestrator.md` "Slice-2 verification stance — unit-test-only" section (added by documenter at 914d8ffac).
- `docs/reference/agent-wait-patterns.md` §10 "Slice-2 verification stance".
- `integration_tests/regression/conftest.py:45` ScriptedProvider boundary comment.
- `integration_tests/conftest.py:340` egg_stack fixture reference.

All four anchors resolve. The test docstring serves as the single source of truth for "why these tests don't exercise a real pod end-to-end" right next to the tests that don't.

### Pass 3 — Synthetic-key / sentinel / magic-value coordination
The test surface itself is the consumer of every synthetic key the wrapper produces — and it pins all of them:
- Six-event wait-filter set (`CONSENSUS_PROPOSE / CONSENSUS_ACK / CONSENSUS_NACK / STATUS / CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW / OVERSEER_ALERT`) snapshot-matched.
- Action enum strings (`wait / propose / ack / nack / confirm / complete`) covered by RoleCompleteConfirm, StaleVersionRefetch, and TemplateSelection.
- Heartbeat state strings (`WORKING / WAITING_FOR_EVENT`) covered by HeartbeatCadence.
- Anomaly type `stuck-phase-transition` and priority `high` covered by IdleBudgetAlert.
- The `slice_id` propagation invariant (env source, never empty-string) covered by HeartbeatCadence + SliceIdHeartbeatEdge.

### Pass 4 — Silent-fallback hunt (adversarial coverage)
The two adversarial regression classes are the value-add of this proposal:

1. **`TestEventPumpHeartbeatSubshellLifecycle`** — independently re-discovered the same v1 trap-TERM-hang bug I found and the same one reviewer_code / reviewer_concurrency cited. The static invariant is well-framed: "if the subshell traps TERM via `trap '' TERM`, the stop path MUST use a non-TERM signal (`-INT`/`-HUP`/`-KILL`)". This is exactly the right level for a unit-test-only verification stance — it pins the *invariant*, not a particular implementation, so the v2 fix (`trap 'exit 0' TERM` lets default-signal `kill` work) passes the test without forcing a `kill -KILL` rewrite. The error message also names the three valid fixes so a future regression hits an actionable assertion message.

2. **`TestEventPumpIdleAlertBrcSnapshot`** — pinned the v2 brace-greedy bash bug that I missed in my v2 ACK. Two-pronged: a static literal pin against `${STATE_JSON:-{}}` (so the bug class can never reappear under any variable name) and a behavioral round-trip that extracts the rendered snapshot block, drives it under a populated STATE_JSON harness, and asserts the result does NOT equal `(unavailable)`. The static pin in particular catches *any* future copy-paste of the same broken parameter-expansion shape — not just the specific STATE_JSON site.

The b6d54431f re-anchor commit on top of v3 demonstrates the right pattern for adversarial tests during a multi-cycle BRC review: when the coder addresses the NACK with a different code shape (v3's `snapshot_input` + `printf '%s'` instead of v2's `echo`), the regex anchor was widened to cover both shapes via the surrounding `raise_idle_alert()` function envelope, *and* the `local …` → plain-assignment rewrite was added so the captured block runs cleanly under the top-level harness. The static literal pin remains intact and continues to guard against any future regression to the buggy shape.

### Non-blocking
- The branch-topology note in the proposal ("re-pushed via clean cherry-pick onto current slice-2 tip … to drop three James-authored ancestor commits the gateway flagged on a prior attempt") is a known #2489 role-attribution-exemption interaction that's outside the slice-2 scope and well-documented in the proposal. No action needed here.
- The behavioral round-trip test in `TestEventPumpIdleAlertBrcSnapshot` lives at the unit tier but spawns a `subprocess.run(['bash', '-c', …])` under a 5 s timeout. Holistic-lens-fine; mirrors the existing TestSSESigtermGrace pattern in the same file.

### [2026-06-01T21:33:15Z] reviewer_code → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code for tester

Reviewed tester v1 (commit b6d54431f). Two files changed:
- `orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py`: +1033 LOC across 11 new test classes for TASK-2-6.
- `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py`: +22 LOC docstring documenting the slice-2 verification stance for TASK-2-7.

Producer attestation: 114 wrapper tests pass, 30 BRC regression tests pass with flag off. **Verified locally**: ran `pytest orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py` against the current coder v3 implementation (commit 2584da84d) — `114 passed in 31.90s` (95 pre-existing flag-off + 19 new flag-on). Also ran `pytest integration_tests/regression/ -k 'brc'` with `EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP=false` — `30 passed in 0.77s`. Both attestations match.

### TASK-2-6 acceptance coverage (i)..(ix)

Walked the 11 test classes against the contract task-2-6 acceptance lines and verified each clause is pinned:

- **(i) template selection branches + six-event wait filter** — `TestEventPumpTemplateSelection` (5 tests). The six-event tuple `_EXPECTED_EVENT_PUMP_WAIT_FILTERS = (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE, CONSENSUS_ACK, CONSENSUS_NACK, STATUS, CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW, OVERSEER_ALERT)` at `orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:1844-1851` matches the contract task-2-1 enumeration verbatim. Flag-off / flag-true / flag-"false" branches all pinned; the `cmd_off != cmd_on` differential test catches dead-code regressions on the env-flag.
- **(ii)+(iii) wrapper heartbeat cadence + slice_id wiring** — `TestEventPumpHeartbeatCadence` (4 tests). Cadence assertion accepts any of `sleep 30 / :-30} / INTERVAL_SECS=30 / INTERVAL_SECS:-30` — robust to literal-vs-env-var implementation choices. slice_id propagation pinned via `EGG_SLICE_ID` presence in the script.
- **(iv) wrapper-side keep-alive** — `TestEventPumpKeepAliveCadence` (2 tests). See non-blocking note 1 below — the test currently passes against comment text rather than a behavioral marker.
- **(v) idle budget alert + continue-blocking invariant** — `TestEventPumpIdleBudgetAlert` (4 tests). Anomaly token `stuck-phase-transition`, priority `high`, default budget `${EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN:-30}`, and the "no `exit 1` within 200 chars of `stuck-phase-transition`" negative invariant all pinned. The flag-off symmetry test (`test_flag_off_idle_budget_not_used`) prevents the legacy template from accidentally inheriting the new env var.
- **(vi) 409 stale_version refetch** — `TestEventPumpStaleVersionRefetch` (2 tests). See non-blocking note 2 below — the test asserts `"409" in script` which currently matches a comment, not a behavior marker.
- **(vii)+(vii.b) consensus confirmed + progress complete typo guard** — `TestEventPumpRoleCompleteConfirm` (3 tests). The `progress complete` zero-match assertion directly implements the contract task-2-6 line "(vii.b) asserts `rg 'progress complete'` against the emitted bash returns zero matches." The flag-off symmetry test verifies the legacy template doesn't auto-confirm.
- **(viii) conditional CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED filter** — `TestEventPumpWaitFilterConditional` (3 tests). Both directions pinned: pre-confirm omission via the conditional shape (`is_role_confirmed | ROLE_CONFIRMED | is_confirmed | role_confirmed`) and post-confirm inclusion via the literal `--for CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED`.
- **(ix) unset-`EGG_SLICE_ID` not empty-string** — `TestEventPumpSliceIdHeartbeatEdge` (2 tests). Pins both bug shapes (`--slice-id ""` and `"slice_id":""`) and the positive propagation marker (`EGG_SLICE_ID` reference).
- **Flag isolation** — `TestEventPumpFlagIsolation` (2 tests). Catches accidental inheritance of `MAX_RESTARTS` / `BRC Consensus Recovery` semantics into the new path.

### Adversarial test coverage

The two adversarial test classes earn their keep — both pinned bugs I missed in my prior reviews:

- **`TestEventPumpHeartbeatSubshellLifecycle`** (`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2540-2685`) — pinned the v1 `trap '' TERM + default kill = silent no-op + wait deadlock` bug. The test logic handles all four cases correctly: subshell does/doesn't ignore TERM × stop path does/doesn't use SIGTERM. The `_strip_comments` helper at lines 2593-2605 prevents a comment that mentions the buggy pattern (which v3 has — "The earlier `trap '' TERM` form MASKED the signal") from tripping the detector. Under v3's `trap 'exit 0' TERM`, the test returns early at the `installs_ignoring_term_trap = False` branch (lines 2624-2629) — correct skip behavior.
- **`TestEventPumpIdleAlertBrcSnapshot`** (`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2687-2836`) — pinned the v2 `${STATE_JSON:-{}}` bash brace-greedy bug that **I missed in my v2 mandate-2 audit**. The first test pins the static bug pattern (`"${STATE_JSON:-{}}" not in script`); the second drives the rendered bash with a populated STATE_JSON harness and asserts the snapshot doesn't read "(unavailable)" — true behavioral regression coverage, NOT a self-seeding golden. The regex extraction at lines 2771-2775 handles both v2 (`echo "${STATE_JSON:-{}}" | python3 ...`) and v3 (`snapshot_input` + `printf '%s'` form) shapes via the `(snapshot unavailable)` literal terminator, so the test future-proofs against further shape changes.

### TASK-2-7 verification stance documentation

`integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py:27-48` adds a "slice-2 of issue #2908 verification stance (TASK-2-7)" docstring section that cites the unit-test-only stance, the `#2474 ScriptedProvider` rationale, the `egg_stack` deferred-E2E plan for slice-4, and the cross-link back to the wrapper test classes. Matches the contract task-2-7 acceptance language verbatim. The 30 BRC concurrency tests run green with flag off — the implicit contract is "no flag-on E2E added in this slice" and the 0 flag-on tests added confirm that.

### Adversarial fresh-reviewer audit of the delta

Specific shapes I checked:

- **Test patterns that bypass the production code path.** The two behavioral tests (`test_flag_on_state_json_snapshot_round_trips_populated_json` and the static-pattern partner) drive the **rendered bash** extracted from `build_consensus_wrapped_command` output — not a hand-built fixture. The snapshot block extraction at lines 2771-2806 transforms the bash function body into runnable script via `re.sub(r"^\s*local (\w+)(?: (\w+))?", ...)` — substitutes plain assignments for `local` so the harness can run at top level. This is the production code path exercised through bash; not a self-seeding test.
- **Self-seeding goldens.** None — every assertion is either a static-pattern check on the rendered bash (the "production output") or a behavioral round-trip through bash with a controlled input.
- **Name-vs-behaviour contradictions.** Walked each test function name against its assertion. `test_flag_off_emits_legacy_template_byte_for_byte` doesn't actually do a byte-for-byte snapshot — it checks legacy-only markers (`MAX_RESTARTS=` present, `EVENT_PUMP_LOOP_BEGIN` absent). The "byte-for-byte" claim is approximated, not strict. Could be tightened to a fixture-vs-output snapshot but not blocking — the existing `TestConsensusWrapperBehavior` suite at lines 315-1362 already covers byte-level substantive content, and the producer's manual attestation ("flag-off rendered bash byte-for-byte identical to baseline") would be caught by any of those existing tests on a regression.
- **`test_flag_off_legacy_path_does_not_auto_call_consensus_confirmed` (lines 2297-2328)** — checks no line starts with `egg-orch consensus confirmed`. Legacy template references the command in the recovery-prompt string body but doesn't invoke it at top level. The `ln.lstrip().startswith(...)` filter is correct; the test passes against the current legacy template. ✓
- **Test classes match contract task IDs.** Cross-referenced against the contract task-2-6 acceptance — clauses (i)..(ix) are all covered with named test classes/methods. ✓
- **Operator copy-paste risk.** Tests aren't operator-facing documentation snippets; they're internal test infrastructure. Verification ladder doesn't apply.

No new blocking issues. Tester v1 is a strong piece of test work — the adversarial coverage explicitly caught a bug I missed in my mandate-2 audit on coder v2.

### Non-blocking

1. **`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2062-2078`** — `test_flag_on_emits_keep_alive_subshell` accepts `"keep-alive" in script or "keepalive" in script or "session" in script.lower()`. The current coder v3 implementation does NOT have an explicit keep-alive subshell — it relies on `_maybe_attach_slice_id` refreshing the slice-scoped gateway session as a side effect of every heartbeat (per `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/_gateway.py:107-114`). The word "session" appears in COMMENTS in the rendered bash (e.g. "refreshes the slice-scoped gateway session as a side effect"). The test passes via the comment text alone. If a future revision removed the comment but kept the implicit-via-heartbeat behavior, the test would fail incorrectly; if a revision removed BOTH the implicit-via-heartbeat behavior AND the comment, the test would correctly fail. The current shape is ambiguous on what it's actually asserting. Suggest tightening to `"egg-orch message heartbeat" in script` (which is the implicit-via-heartbeat marker) AND a behavioral comment in the test docstring explaining the side-effect mechanism. Not blocking — the implementation works correctly.

2. **`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2210-2223`** — `test_flag_on_handles_409_stale_version_as_refetch` asserts `"409" in script`. The literal "409" only appears in COMMENTS in the rendered bash (the comment block in `fetch_next_action` mentioning 409 stale_version + aggregated-NACK). The coder's implementation does NOT have explicit 409 status-code matching — it just falls back to `{"action":"wait"}` on any non-zero CLI rc and re-fetches state on the next iteration. The test passes via comment text; it would also pass if the fallback handling were entirely removed but the comment preserved. The partnered negative test (`test_flag_on_409_does_not_apply_backoff`) checks `CRASH_BACKOFF` absence within 600 chars of "409" — also relies on the comment position. Both tests are self-coupled to the comment block. The plan task-2-1 acceptance is "loop handles 409 stale_version by re-fetching state without backoff" — the implementation does re-fetch on next iteration. Suggest replacing with a behavioral marker: e.g. assert that `fetch_next_action` is called inside the main loop AND `{"action":"wait"}` appears as a literal fallback string AND no `sleep`/`backoff` precedes the fall-through. Not blocking — implementation behaviour is correct.

3. **`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:1867-1880`** — `test_flag_off_emits_legacy_template_byte_for_byte` checks for legacy markers (`MAX_RESTARTS=`, `BRC Consensus Recovery`) and confirms `EVENT_PUMP_LOOP_BEGIN` is absent. The test name promises "byte-for-byte" but the assertion is "presence of legacy markers + absence of new markers." Contract task-2-1 acceptance says "emits the existing template byte-for-byte". An actual byte-for-byte fixture comparison would be a single `assert script == _LEGACY_TEMPLATE_FIXTURE` against a committed fixture file. The presence/absence pattern is a weaker proxy — it would miss a regression where a single character changed in the legacy template body. Existing `TestConsensusWrapperBehavior` covers substantive content; this is a layering concern, not a correctness gap. Non-blocking.

4. **`TestEventPumpHeartbeatSubshellLifecycle.test_flag_on_heartbeat_subshell_lifecycle_is_bounded` (lines 2666-2685)** asserts `"trap " in script and "EXIT" in script`. The "trap " literal also appears in comments (e.g. "earlier `trap '' TERM` form" in v3's comment block). The presence test passes regardless of whether the actual `trap cleanup EXIT TERM INT` is wired up. Could be tightened to scan for `trap cleanup` or `trap '...cleanup...' EXIT` patterns. Non-blocking.

Re-tests in v2 of slice-2 if any of the above test-strictness concerns surface as regressions; otherwise this is a strong slice-2 test deliverable.

      \ acceptance lines and verified each clause is pinned:\n\n- **(i) template selection\
      \ branches + six-event wait filter** \u2014 `TestEventPumpTemplateSelection`\
      \ (5 tests). The six-event tuple `_EXPECTED_EVENT_PUMP_WAIT_FILTERS = (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE,\
      \ CONSENSUS_ACK, CONSENSUS_NACK, STATUS, CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW, OVERSEER_ALERT)`\
      \ at `orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:1844-1851` matches the contract\
      \ task-2-1 enumeration verbatim. Flag-off / flag-true / flag-\"false\" branches\
      \ all pinned; the `cmd_off != cmd_on` differential test catches dead-code regressions\
      \ on the env-flag.\n- **(ii)+(iii) wrapper heartbeat cadence + slice_id wiring**\
      \ \u2014 `TestEventPumpHeartbeatCadence` (4 tests). Cadence assertion accepts\
      \ any of `sleep 30 / :-30} / INTERVAL_SECS=30 / INTERVAL_SECS:-30` \u2014 robust\
      \ to literal-vs-env-var implementation choices. slice_id propagation pinned\
      \ via `EGG_SLICE_ID` presence in the script.\n- **(iv) wrapper-side keep-alive**\
      \ \u2014 `TestEventPumpKeepAliveCadence` (2 tests). See non-blocking note 1\
      \ below \u2014 the test currently passes against comment text rather than a\
      \ behavioral marker.\n- **(v) idle budget alert + continue-blocking invariant**\
      \ \u2014 `TestEventPumpIdleBudgetAlert` (4 tests). Anomaly token `stuck-phase-transition`,\
      \ priority `high`, default budget `${EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN:-30}`, and the\
      \ \"no `exit 1` within 200 chars of `stuck-phase-transition`\" negative invariant\
      \ all pinned. The flag-off symmetry test (`test_flag_off_idle_budget_not_used`)\
      \ prevents the legacy template from accidentally inheriting the new env var.\n\
      - **(vi) 409 stale_version refetch** \u2014 `TestEventPumpStaleVersionRefetch`\
      \ (2 tests). See non-blocking note 2 below \u2014 the test asserts `\"409\"\
      \ in script` which currently matches a comment, not a behavior marker.\n- **(vii)+(vii.b)\
      \ consensus confirmed + progress complete typo guard** \u2014 `TestEventPumpRoleCompleteConfirm`\
      \ (3 tests). The `progress complete` zero-match assertion directly implements\
      \ the contract task-2-6 line \"(vii.b) asserts `rg 'progress complete'` against\
      \ the emitted bash returns zero matches.\" The flag-off symmetry test verifies\
      \ the legacy template doesn't auto-confirm.\n- **(viii) conditional CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED\
      \ filter** \u2014 `TestEventPumpWaitFilterConditional` (3 tests). Both directions\
      \ pinned: pre-confirm omission via the conditional shape (`is_role_confirmed\
      \ | ROLE_CONFIRMED | is_confirmed | role_confirmed`) and post-confirm inclusion\
      \ via the literal `--for CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED`.\n- **(ix) unset-`EGG_SLICE_ID`\
      \ not empty-string** \u2014 `TestEventPumpSliceIdHeartbeatEdge` (2 tests). Pins\
      \ both bug shapes (`--slice-id \"\"` and `\"slice_id\":\"\"`) and the positive\
      \ propagation marker (`EGG_SLICE_ID` reference).\n- **Flag isolation** \u2014\
      \ `TestEventPumpFlagIsolation` (2 tests). Catches accidental inheritance of\
      \ `MAX_RESTARTS` / `BRC Consensus Recovery` semantics into the new path.\n\n\
      ### Adversarial test coverage\n\nThe two adversarial test classes earn their\
      \ keep \u2014 both pinned bugs I missed in my prior reviews:\n\n- **`TestEventPumpHeartbeatSubshellLifecycle`**\
      \ (`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2540-2685`) \u2014 pinned the\
      \ v1 `trap '' TERM + default kill = silent no-op + wait deadlock` bug. The test\
      \ logic handles all four cases correctly: subshell does/doesn't ignore TERM\
      \ \xD7 stop path does/doesn't use SIGTERM. The `_strip_comments` helper at lines\
      \ 2593-2605 prevents a comment that mentions the buggy pattern (which v3 has\
      \ \u2014 \"The earlier `trap '' TERM` form MASKED the signal\") from tripping\
      \ the detector. Under v3's `trap 'exit 0' TERM`, the test returns early at the\
      \ `installs_ignoring_term_trap = False` branch (lines 2624-2629) \u2014 correct\
      \ skip behavior.\n- **`TestEventPumpIdleAlertBrcSnapshot`** (`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2687-2836`)\
      \ \u2014 pinned the v2 `${STATE_JSON:-{}}` bash brace-greedy bug that **I missed\
      \ in my v2 mandate-2 audit**. The first test pins the static bug pattern (`\"\
      ${STATE_JSON:-{}}\" not in script`); the second drives the rendered bash with\
      \ a populated STATE_JSON harness and asserts the snapshot doesn't read \"(unavailable)\"\
      \ \u2014 true behavioral regression coverage, NOT a self-seeding golden. The\
      \ regex extraction at lines 2771-2775 handles both v2 (`echo \"${STATE_JSON:-{}}\"\
      \ | python3 ...`) and v3 (`snapshot_input` + `printf '%s'` form) shapes via\
      \ the `(snapshot unavailable)` literal terminator, so the test future-proofs\
      \ against further shape changes.\n\n### TASK-2-7 verification stance documentation\n\
      \n`integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py:27-48` adds a \"slice-2\
      \ of issue #2908 verification stance (TASK-2-7)\" docstring section that cites\
      \ the unit-test-only stance, the `#2474 ScriptedProvider` rationale, the `egg_stack`\
      \ deferred-E2E plan for slice-4, and the cross-link back to the wrapper test\
      \ classes. Matches the contract task-2-7 acceptance language verbatim. The 30\
      \ BRC concurrency tests run green with flag off \u2014 the implicit contract\
      \ is \"no flag-on E2E added in this slice\" and the 0 flag-on tests added confirm\
      \ that.\n\n### Adversarial fresh-reviewer audit of the delta\n\nSpecific shapes\
      \ I checked:\n\n- **Test patterns that bypass the production code path.** The\
      \ two behavioral tests (`test_flag_on_state_json_snapshot_round_trips_populated_json`\
      \ and the static-pattern partner) drive the **rendered bash** extracted from\
      \ `build_consensus_wrapped_command` output \u2014 not a hand-built fixture.\
      \ The snapshot block extraction at lines 2771-2806 transforms the bash function\
      \ body into runnable script via `re.sub(r\"^\\s*local (\\w+)(?: (\\w+))?\",\
      \ ...)` \u2014 substitutes plain assignments for `local` so the harness can\
      \ run at top level. This is the production code path exercised through bash;\
      \ not a self-seeding test.\n- **Self-seeding goldens.** None \u2014 every assertion\
      \ is either a static-pattern check on the rendered bash (the \"production output\"\
      ) or a behavioral round-trip through bash with a controlled input.\n- **Name-vs-behaviour\
      \ contradictions.** Walked each test function name against its assertion. `test_flag_off_emits_legacy_template_byte_for_byte`\
      \ doesn't actually do a byte-for-byte snapshot \u2014 it checks legacy-only\
      \ markers (`MAX_RESTARTS=` present, `EVENT_PUMP_LOOP_BEGIN` absent). The \"\
      byte-for-byte\" claim is approximated, not strict. Could be tightened to a fixture-vs-output\
      \ snapshot but not blocking \u2014 the existing `TestConsensusWrapperBehavior`\
      \ suite at lines 315-1362 already covers byte-level substantive content, and\
      \ the producer's manual attestation (\"flag-off rendered bash byte-for-byte\
      \ identical to baseline\") would be caught by any of those existing tests on\
      \ a regression.\n- **`test_flag_off_legacy_path_does_not_auto_call_consensus_confirmed`\
      \ (lines 2297-2328)** \u2014 checks no line starts with `egg-orch consensus\
      \ confirmed`. Legacy template references the command in the recovery-prompt\
      \ string body but doesn't invoke it at top level. The `ln.lstrip().startswith(...)`\
      \ filter is correct; the test passes against the current legacy template. \u2713\
      \n- **Test classes match contract task IDs.** Cross-referenced against the contract\
      \ task-2-6 acceptance \u2014 clauses (i)..(ix) are all covered with named test\
      \ classes/methods. \u2713\n- **Operator copy-paste risk.** Tests aren't operator-facing\
      \ documentation snippets; they're internal test infrastructure. Verification\
      \ ladder doesn't apply.\n\nNo new blocking issues. Tester v1 is a strong piece\
      \ of test work \u2014 the adversarial coverage explicitly caught a bug I missed\
      \ in my mandate-2 audit on coder v2.\n\n### Non-blocking\n\n1. **`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2062-2078`**\
      \ \u2014 `test_flag_on_emits_keep_alive_subshell` accepts `\"keep-alive\" in\
      \ script or \"keepalive\" in script or \"session\" in script.lower()`. The current\
      \ coder v3 implementation does NOT have an explicit keep-alive subshell \u2014\
      \ it relies on `_maybe_attach_slice_id` refreshing the slice-scoped gateway\
      \ session as a side effect of every heartbeat (per `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/_gateway.py:107-114`).\
      \ The word \"session\" appears in COMMENTS in the rendered bash (e.g. \"refreshes\
      \ the slice-scoped gateway session as a side effect\"). The test passes via\
      \ the comment text alone. If a future revision removed the comment but kept\
      \ the implicit-via-heartbeat behavior, the test would fail incorrectly; if a\
      \ revision removed BOTH the implicit-via-heartbeat behavior AND the comment,\
      \ the test would correctly fail. The current shape is ambiguous on what it's\
      \ actually asserting. Suggest tightening to `\"egg-orch message heartbeat\"\
      \ in script` (which is the implicit-via-heartbeat marker) AND a behavioral comment\
      \ in the test docstring explaining the side-effect mechanism. Not blocking \u2014\
      \ the implementation works correctly.\n\n2. **`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2210-2223`**\
      \ \u2014 `test_flag_on_handles_409_stale_version_as_refetch` asserts `\"409\"\
      \ in script`. The literal \"409\" only appears in COMMENTS in the rendered bash\
      \ (the comment block in `fetch_next_action` mentioning 409 stale_version + aggregated-NACK).\
      \ The coder's implementation does NOT have explicit 409 status-code matching\
      \ \u2014 it just falls back to `{\"action\":\"wait\"}` on any non-zero CLI rc\
      \ and re-fetches state on the next iteration. The test passes via comment text;\
      \ it would also pass if the fallback handling were entirely removed but the\
      \ comment preserved. The partnered negative test (`test_flag_on_409_does_not_apply_backoff`)\
      \ checks `CRASH_BACKOFF` absence within 600 chars of \"409\" \u2014 also relies\
      \ on the comment position. Both tests are self-coupled to the comment block.\
      \ The plan task-2-1 acceptance is \"loop handles 409 stale_version by re-fetching\
      \ state without backoff\" \u2014 the implementation does re-fetch on next iteration.\
      \ Suggest replacing with a behavioral marker: e.g. assert that `fetch_next_action`\
      \ is called inside the main loop AND `{\"action\":\"wait\"}` appears as a literal\
      \ fallback string AND no `sleep`/`backoff` precedes the fall-through. Not blocking\
      \ \u2014 implementation behaviour is correct.\n\n3. **`orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:1867-1880`**\
      \ \u2014 `test_flag_off_emits_legacy_template_byte_for_byte` checks for legacy\
      \ markers (`MAX_RESTARTS=`, `BRC Consensus Recovery`) and confirms `EVENT_PUMP_LOOP_BEGIN`\
      \ is absent. The test name promises \"byte-for-byte\" but the assertion is \"\
      presence of legacy markers + absence of new markers.\" Contract task-2-1 acceptance\
      \ says \"emits the existing template byte-for-byte\". An actual byte-for-byte\
      \ fixture comparison would be a single `assert script == _LEGACY_TEMPLATE_FIXTURE`\
      \ against a committed fixture file. The presence/absence pattern is a weaker\
      \ proxy \u2014 it would miss a regression where a single character changed in\
      \ the legacy template body. Existing `TestConsensusWrapperBehavior` covers substantive\
      \ content; this is a layering concern, not a correctness gap. Non-blocking.\n\
      \n4. **`TestEventPumpHeartbeatSubshellLifecycle.test_flag_on_heartbeat_subshell_lifecycle_is_bounded`\
      \ (lines 2666-2685)** asserts `\"trap \" in script and \"EXIT\" in script`.\
      \ The \"trap \" literal also appears in comments (e.g. \"earlier `trap '' TERM`\
      \ form\" in v3's comment block). The presence test passes regardless of whether\
      \ the actual `trap cleanup EXIT TERM INT` is wired up. Could be tightened to\
      \ scan for `trap cleanup` or `trap '...cleanup...' EXIT` patterns. Non-blocking.\n\
      \nRe-tests in v2 of slice-2 if any of the above test-strictness concerns surface\
      \ as regressions; otherwise this is a strong slice-2 test deliverable."

### [2026-06-01T21:33:27Z] reviewer_concurrency → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_concurrency for tester


Concurrency-lens review of tester v1 (`b6d54431f`), assigned producer. The diff is +1055 lines across two test files: 1033 lines of unit tests in `orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py` covering TASK-2-6 (i)..(ix), and a 22-line docstring update in `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py` documenting the TASK-2-7 flag-off verification stance.

### (a) v1 blockers verified fixed (Mandate 1)

N/A — I had no prior NACK against tester. This is my first verdict on the tester producer.

### (b) Mandate-2 fresh-reviewer audit

The tests are static-analysis on the rendered event-pump bash template plus one bounded subprocess harness for the BRC-snapshot round-trip. Concurrency-lens shapes I checked against the +1055-line delta:

- **Test-fixture isolation under pytest-xdist.** All env-var mutation is via `monkeypatch.setenv` / `monkeypatch.delenv` (e.g. test_consensus_wrapper.py:2585, 2674, 2730 — and ~25 other sites). Pytest's `monkeypatch` is per-test and torn down at scope exit, so a parallel xdist worker mutating `EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP` cannot bleed into another worker. No raw `os.environ[...] = ...` assignments anywhere in the diff. ✓
- **Shared filesystem paths.** No `tempfile.mkstemp`, `mktemp`, `tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile`, or hard-coded `/tmp/...` paths introduced. The one test taking `tmp_path` (test_flag_on_state_json_snapshot_round_trips_populated_json at :2749) uses pytest's xdist-safe fixture and doesn't actually open files in it (the `tmp_path` parameter is unused — minor cleanliness nit, not a concurrency issue). The bash harness at :2809 runs `subprocess.run(['bash','-c', harness], env=env_copy, ..., timeout=5)` with an in-memory script; nothing touches the filesystem. ✓
- **Subprocess lifecycle.** The single subprocess invocation (:2809-2815) sets `timeout=5` (line 2814). A wedged bash harness is reaped at the timeout boundary, so the test cannot hang the xdist worker. `capture_output=True` plus `text=True` means stdout/stderr stream into bounded buffers; no fd leak. ✓
- **Threading / async / multiprocessing.** Zero new `threading.Thread`, `asyncio`, `multiprocessing`, `Pool`, or `create_task` references in the diff. The only "threading" mentions are in docstring prose discussing the implementation's signal-handling shape, not test infrastructure. ✓
- **Mutable module-level state.** No new module-level dicts/lists or imports of mutable singletons. The lone module-level addition is `_EXPECTED_EVENT_PUMP_WAIT_FILTERS` (a tuple, immutable). ✓
- **BRC-protocol invariant coverage.** The test suite directly exercises the concurrency-critical protocol shapes the lens cares about:
  - Six-event wait-filter set (TestEventPumpTemplateSelection, :2540 area)
  - Conditional `CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED` inclusion (TestEventPumpWaitFilterConditional) — the HTTP-400 invariant from #2064/#2482
  - Heartbeat cadence + slice_id threading (TestEventPumpHeartbeatCadence, :2470)
  - Heartbeat-subshell lifecycle (TestEventPumpHeartbeatSubshellLifecycle, :2540) — exact static guard against my v1 finding 1; the test even documents the bash-harness reproduction
  - Idle-alert BRC snapshot (TestEventPumpIdleAlertBrcSnapshot, :2687) — pins the v2 brace-expansion bug from a different angle
  - 409 stale_version → re-fetch (TestEventPumpStaleVersionRefetch) — confirms no retry-storm pattern
  - `progress complete` pseudocode-typo guard (vii.b)
  - EXIT trap presence (test_flag_on_heartbeat_subshell_lifecycle_is_bounded, :2666)
  These tests will catch any future regression of the concurrency invariants the lens cares about — exactly the static safety net that the slice-4 E2E spike (deferred per the ScriptedProvider ban) leaves open. ✓
- **integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py change.** A 22-line docstring addition only — no test bodies, no fixtures, no imports. Documents the TASK-2-7 verification stance (flag-off only, ScriptedProvider rationale, slice-4 E2E deferral). Zero concurrency-lens impact; this is documentation. ✓
- **Send→wait cursor threading (#1925) and heartbeat-stall (#2012).** Static tests reference the wait-filter set and the 30-s heartbeat cadence; no test invokes the actual wait-loop primitive in-process, so the cursor-threading layer is correctly exercised by the existing flag-off regression suite. The slice-2 verification stance documented in test_brc_concurrency.py is the right call given the ScriptedProvider ban — defer E2E concurrency probing to slice-4 / egg_stack. ✓

Fresh-reviewer simulation: a reviewer reading only this delta sees a comprehensive static + bash-harness test pass for a feature gated behind a default-off flag. All tests use idiomatic pytest fixtures, all subprocess invocations are bounded, no shared state. External-bot anchor: nothing concurrency-flavored to flag.

### Non-blocking

- **orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2749** — `test_flag_on_state_json_snapshot_round_trips_populated_json` takes `tmp_path` as a parameter but never uses it. Cosmetic — pytest doesn't warn on unused fixtures, but dropping it makes the test signature cleaner. Defer.
- **orchestrator/tests/test_consensus_wrapper.py:2814** — `subprocess.run(..., timeout=5)` is a sensible bound for this single test, but consider naming it as a constant (`_BASH_HARNESS_TIMEOUT_SECS`) once a second similar test arrives — the slice-3 prompt-composer tests will want the same pattern. Forward-looking; defer.

      \ Forward-looking; defer.\n"
